. The solid-blue curve is only with the evolved gluon GPD. The black data points are from H1, ZEUS
Aaron et al. 2010
.

V.1
Comparison to experiment for
\phi

In Table
1
, we present the parameters for the AdS construction with a soft wall:
\lambda=11.243
,
\tau=3
,
\tilde{\kappa}_{T,S}=0.388~\text{GeV}
, and
\tilde{\kappa}_{N,V}=0.402~\text{GeV}
, as well as the normalization constants
\mathcal{N}_{t}=0.200
, and
\mathcal{N}_{s}=0
. We then illustrate the longitudinal cross section for the electroproduction of phi mesons (
\mathcal{N}_{t}=0.200
in Eq. (
V.88
)) as a function of
W=\sqrt{s}
and
Q^{2}
in Fig.
11
and Fig.
12
, respectively. The values of the physical parameters used are
m_{N}=0.94~\text{GeV}
,
m_{\phi}=1.019~\text{GeV}
, and
f^{\prime}_{\phi}=f_{\phi}=0.233~\text{GeV}
.

It is important to note that the solid-blue curve represents the evolved gluon GPD only, as the strange singlet quark GPD has been set to zero. Additionally, we assume the phi meson’s coupling to the proton (
g_{\phi pp}=0
) to be zero, effectively setting the s-channel background contribution to zero. Experimental data points are included from various sources: CLAS 2008 (orange)
Santoro et al. 2008
, CLAS 2001 (brown)
Lukashin et al. 2001
, HERMES 2000 (cyan)
Kroll 2015
, ZEUS (grey)
Chekanov et al. 2005
, H1 (pink)
Adloff et al. 2000b
, and H1, ZEUS (black)
Aaron et al. 2010
. Figures 5 and 7 from
Kroll 2015
and
Favart et al. 2016
, respectively, also display these data points.

VI
Electroproduction of longitudinal
\rho^{+}
meson with evolved valence quark GPDs: a comparison to experiment

The electroproduction of longitudinal charged rho meson (
\rho^{+}
) in terms of quark DAs and quark GPDs is given by (see Eq.219 with Eq.222 and Eq.223 in
Goeke et al. 2001
)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle-e\times{C_{F}\over N_{c}}\times{1\over 2}\times(4\pi\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}))\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\left\{A_{\rho^{+}\,n}\,h^{+}\,+\,B_{\rho^{+}\,n}\,e^{+}\right\}\,,

(VI.89)

where
\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)
is the charged rho meson (
\rho^{+}
) distribution amplitude (DA),
and

\displaystyle A_{\rho^{+}\,n}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{-1}^{1}dx\;{\left(H^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{{{Q_{u}}\over{\xi-x-i\epsilon}}-{{Q_{d}}\over{\xi+x-i\epsilon}}\right\}

\displaystyle B_{\rho^{+}\,n}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{-1}^{1}dx\;{\left(E^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,E^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{{{Q_{u}}\over{\xi-x-i\epsilon}}-{{Q_{d}}\over{\xi+x-i\epsilon}}\right\}\,.

(VI.90)

Using
C_{F}=\frac{N_{c}^{2}-1}{2N_{c}}
, and
Q_{u}=+2/3\,(Q_{d}=-1/3)
, we can rewrite (
VI
-
VI
) as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle-e\times{1\over 2}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\right)\times 4\pi\times\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})

(VI.91)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times u(p_{2})\gamma^{+}u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\;{\left(H_{valence}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H_{valence}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{\frac{\xi}{2x}\times\frac{2x}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dx\;{\left(H_{singlet}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H_{singlet}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{\frac{1}{6}\times\frac{2x}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\times Q_{u}\left(H^{u}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle-i

\displaystyle\pi\times Q_{d}\left(H^{u}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)\Bigg]\,,

where we have defined
H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=H^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})+H^{q}(-x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
,
H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=H^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})-H^{q}(-x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
, and we have ignored the Pauli contribution for
-t\ll 4m_{N}^{2}
.

Ignoring the singlet quark GPDs contribution in (
VI.91
) by assuming
H^{u}_{singlet}=H^{d}_{singlet}
, the longitudinal electroproduction of
\rho^{+}
meson with only evolved valence quark GPDs is given by

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

(VI.92)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e\times{1\over 2}\times{1\over 2}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\right)\times 4\pi\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\,\frac{\xi}{2x}\times\frac{2x}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\times\left(H_{valence}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H_{valence}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\times Q_{u}\left(H^{u}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)-i\pi\times Q_{d}\left(H^{u}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle-e\times{1\over 2}\times{1\over 2}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\right)\times 4\pi\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\frac{1}{\xi}\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,\frac{\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)}{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]\times\left(\mathbb{F}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\mathbb{F}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\times Q_{u}\left(H^{u}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)-i\pi\times Q_{d}\left(H^{u}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,H^{d}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\,\approx\,

\displaystyle-e\times{1\over 2}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\right)\times 4\pi\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]\times\left(\mathbb{F}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\mathbb{F}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\,,

with odd
j=1,3,...
, and in the last line we have used
\eta\sim\xi
. Again, the
i\pi H^{u,d}
contributions vanish
for
\xi=\pm\eta
. We can also rewrite (
VI.92
) more compactly as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\approx-e\times f_{V}^{+}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j)\times\left(\mathbb{F}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\mathbb{F}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where we have defined

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{1\over 2}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\right)\times 4\pi\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times I_{q(valence)}(j)\,,

(VI.94)

and the integrals (with
\tilde{x}=x/\xi
)

\displaystyle f_{V}^{+}=\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\rho^{+}}(z)}\over z}\,,\qquad\qquad I_{q(valence)}(j)=\left[\int_{0}^{1}d\tilde{x}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\tilde{x}\right)\right]\,,

(VI.95)

VI.1
Final amplitude for
\rho^{+}

Figure 13:
Illustration of the integration contour
\mathbb{C}
in the complex j-plane used for computing the contour integral (
G
), with a branch cut displayed for
\text{Re(j)}\leq j_{0q}=1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}
.

The resummation by j-contour for the valence quark GPDs contribution (
VI
) is similar to the resummation for the singlet quark and gluon GPDs (albeit using a different integration contour shown in Fig.
13
), and the detailed derivation is given in Appendix
G
. Here we only provide the final answer.

After the resummation of (
VI
) using the contour integral (
G
) with the integration contour
\mathbb{C}
in the complex j-plane, shown in Fig.
13
, and
using the result for
\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]
given in (
G
)
and
\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]
given in (
G.247
), the full complex amplitude with the evolved valence quark GPDs is given by

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\propto\Bigg(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\Bigg)\times\Bigg[(\sqrt{\lambda}/4\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{1}{\xi}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)\times e^{-\tilde{\tau}/\sqrt{\lambda}}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}

\displaystyle+i\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0q}}}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/4\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\Bigg]\times\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\propto\Bigg(\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})-\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\Bigg)\times(-1)\times f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)}{\xi^{1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}}}+\frac{i}{\xi^{1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}}}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\propto{\cal A}^{valence}_{\rho^{+}}(s,t,Q)\times\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

(VI.96)

where we have absorbed
e\times\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/4\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}
into the normalization constant as it varies slowly with
s
. We have also defined the normalized and flavor separated quark conformal moments as

\displaystyle\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u+d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.151\times\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})}{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j=3(valence)}(\xi=0,t=0;\mu^{2}=4~\text{GeV}^{2})}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u-d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.070\times\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})}{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j=3(valence)}(\xi=0,t=0;\mu^{2}=4~\text{GeV}^{2})}\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\times\left(\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u+d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})+\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u-d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\,,

\displaystyle\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\times\left(\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u+d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})-\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u-d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\,,

(VI.97)

where the normalization coefficients are fixed using the lattice data in
Hagler et al. 2008
, and
\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})=\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})
is given in (
III.19
). Note that we have used the average value of
A_{3}^{u+d}(-t=0)=\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u+d}_{j=3(valence)}(\xi=0,t=0;\mu^{2}=4~\text{GeV}^{2})=0.151
from Table XV and Table XVI of
Hagler et al. 2008
. We have also used the value of
A_{3}^{u-d}(-t=0)=\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{F}}}^{u-d}_{j=3(valence)}(\xi=0,t=0;\mu^{2}=4~\text{GeV}^{2}=0.070
from Table IX of
Hagler et al. 2008
.

VI.2
Differential and total cross sections for
\rho^{+}

Finally, using the valence quark GPDs contribution (
VI.1
), we have the total longitudinal differential cross section for electroproduction of
\rho^{+}
meson

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma_{L}(\rho^{+})}{dt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi s^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\rm spin}\Big|\mathcal{N}_{t}\times\mathcal{A}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}^{LL(valence)}\Big|^{2}

(VI.98)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi s^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\Big|\mathcal{N}_{t}\times\mathcal{A}_{\rho^{+}}^{valence}\Big|^{2}\,.

We also calculate the total cross section using the optical theorem as

\displaystyle\sigma_{L}(\rho^{+})=\left(\frac{16\pi\,\frac{d\sigma_{L}(\rho^{+})}{dt}}{1+\rho}\right)^{1/2}_{t=0}\,,

(VI.99)

where
\rho
is the ratio of the real and imaginary part of our total complex amplitude. We have compared our total cross section (
VI.99
) to the experimental data in Fig.
14
. We fix the value of the normalization parameter
\mathcal{N}_{t}
by the charged rho meson data as detailed in the next subsection below.

(a)
Q^{2}=1-1.5
GeV
2

(b)
Q^{2}=1.5-2.0
GeV
2

(c)
Q^{2}=2.0-2.5
GeV
2

(d)
Q^{2}=2.5-3.0

(e)
Q^{2}=3.0-3.5
GeV
2

(f)
Q^{2}=3.5-4.0

(g)
Q^{2}=4.0-4.5
GeV
2

Figure 14:
Longitudinal cross section for the electroproduction of charged rho mesons (
\rho^{+}
) versus
W=\sqrt{s}
in GeV.The blue curve is with the evolved valence up and down quark GPDs (i.e., (
VI.99
).
The black data points are from 5.776 GeV CLAS
Fradi 2011
.

VI.3
Comparison to experiment for
\rho^{+}

In this section, we present our result for the electroproduction of charged rho mesons (
\rho^{+}
) and its comparison to experimental data. First, we list the parameters used in our AdS construction with a soft wall, namely
\lambda=11.243
,
\tau=3
,
\tilde{\kappa}_{T,S}=0.388~\text{GeV}
,
\tilde{\kappa}_{N,V}=0.402~\text{GeV}
, and the normalization constants
\mathcal{N}_{t}=724.513
and
\mathcal{N}_{s}=0
, which are given in Table
1
.

Next, we show the longitudinal cross section for the electroproduction of
\rho^{+}
mesons versus
W=\sqrt{s}
in GeV in Fig.
14
. In these figures, we have used the evolved valence up and down quark GPDs (i.e., (
VI.99
)) with
\mathcal{N}_{t}=724.513
. We have also used the values of the physical parameters
m_{N}=0.94~\text{GeV}
,
m_{\rho^{0,+}}=0.775~\text{GeV}
, and
f^{\prime}_{\rho^{0,+}}=f_{\rho^{0,+}}=0.216~\text{GeV}
. It is important to note that we have assumed that the coupling of the
\rho^{+}
meson to the proton and neutron (
g_{\rho^{+}pn}
) is zero and we have set the s-channel contribution to zero, that is,
\mathcal{N}_{s}=0
.

In the comparison of our results with experimental data, in Fig.
14
, the black data points are from the 5.776 GeV CLAS experiment
Fradi 2011
(see Fig.4 of
Fradi 2011
).

VII
Summary and Conclusion

In this paper, we have presented a framework for constructing General Parton Distributions (GPDs) at low-x and finite skewness using holographic QCD in the double limit of large
N_{c}
and strong gauge coupling. We have first constructed holographic amplitudes for exclusive electroproduction processes, dominated by bulk spin-j gravitons and vector or axial meson exchanges in AdS, which are dual to Pomerons and Reggeons in QCD, respectively. By comparing the holographic exclusive amplitudes with the corresponding exclusive amplitudes in QCD, which are based on factorization theorems, we have extracted the spin-j conformal moments of GPDs and constructed the GPDs.

Using the holographically fixed conformal moments of GPDs at the initial or low renormalization scale
\mu\sim\mu_{0}\sim 1.8
GeV, we have used one-loop pQCD evolution equations to obtain their counterparts at a higher renormalization scale
\mu>\mu_{0}
, which enhances the perturbative partonic content at low-x in addition to the nonperturbative one captured by the holographic construction at the low renormalization scale.

We have used our evolved GPDs to specifically determine the exclusive longitudinal electroproduction of neutral rho (
\rho^{0}
), phi (
\phi
), and charged rho (
\rho^{+}
) mesons, using the factorization theorems in QCD, and compare them to the existing data from CLAS, HERMES, and H1 experiments. Specifically, in the data analysis of the longitudinal electroproduction of
\rho^{0}
, we have found that the holographic GPDs (similar to previous GPD models) alone cannot explain the low
\sqrt{s}
data, and we have accounted for the non-perturbative physics coming from the s-channel. We have successfully determined such non-perturbative contribution in the s-channel using holographic QCD and found that its contribution at low
\sqrt{s}
is in good agreement with the experimental data. We expect the holographic s-channel contribution to play a key role in the future experimental extraction of GPDs at JLab and future EIC.

Our extracted quark and gluon GPDs for fixed skewness
\eta
provide explicit and detailed dependence on the kinematical variables
t
and
\eta
, with their moments satisfying all the polynomiality conditions in
\eta
. They should prove useful for extracting the GPDs using global data analysis and carry detailed tomographic information about the partonic distributions inside a nucleon after Fourier transforming to coordinate space with respect to
t
.

Additionally, our framework can be extended to longitudinal heavier vector mesons such as
J/\Psi
and
\Upsilon
, and yields detailed predictions for exclusive processes such as the electroproduction of photons in DVCS or the longitudinal electroproduction of single and double axial mesons.

In conclusion, this paper provides a powerful framework for constructing GPDs at low-x and finite skewness using holographic QCD in the large
N_{c}
limit. We have demonstrated the potential of this approach for analyzing exclusive electroproduction processes and provided useful results for future experimental studies and global data analyses.

K.M. is supported by the U.S. Department of Energy, Office of Science, Office of Nuclear Physics, contract no. DE-AC02-06CH11357, and an LDRD initiative at Argonne National Laboratory under Project No. 2020-0020. I.Z. is supported by the Office of Science, U.S. Department of Energy under Contract No. DE-FG-88ER40388.

Appendix A
Notations

Here, we summarize the key notations used for the GPDs and their conformal (Gegenbauer) moments:

\displaystyle H^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{axial quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle H^{g}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{gluon GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle H_{\text{valence}}^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{valence quark GPDs, which are }H^{q}(x)+H^{q}(-x)\,,

\displaystyle H_{\text{singlet}}^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{singlet quark GPDs, which are }H^{q}(x)-H^{q}(-x)\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\text{valence}}^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{valence axial quark GPDs, which are }\widetilde{H}^{q}(x)-\widetilde{H}^{q}(-x)\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\text{singlet}}^{q}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\text{singlet axial quark GPDs, which are }\widetilde{H}^{q}(x)+\widetilde{H}^{q}(-x)\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(\text{valence})}

\displaystyle\equiv\text{conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of valence quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(\text{singlet})}

\displaystyle\equiv\text{conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of singlet quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(\text{valence})}

\displaystyle\equiv\text{conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of valence axial quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(\text{singlet})}

\displaystyle\equiv\text{conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of singlet axial quark GPDs}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}

\displaystyle\equiv\text{conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of gluon GPD}.

Appendix B
Kinematics

Figure 15:
Illustration of the kinematic variables defined in (
B.100
) - (
B.106
).

Here, we summarize the pertinent kinematics for the GPDs, see Fig.
15
.
Following
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
, we define

\tilde{q}=\frac{1}{2}(q+q^{\prime})\,,\qquad p=\frac{1}{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})\,,\qquad\Delta=p_{1}-p_{2}=q^{\prime}-q\,.

(B.100)

\tilde{q}^{2}=-\tilde{Q}^{2}\,,\qquad\xi=\frac{\tilde{Q}^{2}}{2p\cdot\tilde{q}}\,,\qquad\eta=\frac{\Delta\cdot\tilde{q}}{2p\cdot\tilde{q}}\,.

(B.101)

Q^{2}=-q^{2}\,,\qquad Q^{\prime 2}=-q^{\prime 2}\,,\qquad x_{\rm B}=\frac{{Q}^{2}}{2p_{1}\cdot q}\,,

(B.102)

\tilde{Q}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(Q^{2}+Q^{\prime 2}+\frac{\Delta^{2}}{2}\right)\,,

(B.103)

\xi=\frac{Q^{2}+Q^{\prime 2}+\Delta^{2}/2}{2Q^{2}/x_{\rm B}-Q^{2}+Q^{\prime 2}+\Delta^{2}}\,,\qquad\eta=\frac{Q^{2}-Q^{\prime 2}}{2Q^{2}/x_{\rm B}-Q^{2}+Q^{\prime 2}+\Delta^{2}}\,,

(B.104)

Q^{\prime 2}=\left(1-\frac{\eta}{\xi}\right)\tilde{Q}^{2}-\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}\,,\qquad Q^{2}=\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\xi}\right)\tilde{Q}^{2}-\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}\,,

(B.105)

and

x_{\rm B}=\frac{(\xi+\eta)\tilde{Q}^{2}-\xi\Delta^{2}/4}{(1+\eta)\tilde{Q}^{2}-\xi\Delta^{2}/2}\,.

(B.106)

We also introduce a pair of
light-cone vectors such that
n^{2}=n^{\ast 2}=0
and
n\cdot n^{\ast}\equiv n^{\mu}n^{\ast}_{\mu}=1
. They can be chosen as

n^{\mu}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,0,0,-1)\,,\qquad n^{\ast\mu}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,0,0,1)\,.

(B.107)

Any four-vector
a^{\mu}
can be decomposed into its light-cone components as

a^{\mu}=a^{+}n^{\ast\mu}+a^{-}n^{\mu}+a^{\mu}_{\perp}\,,

(B.108)

with

a^{+}\equiv a\cdot n=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(a^{0}+a^{z})\,,\qquad a^{-}\equiv a\cdot n^{\ast}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(a^{0}-a^{z})\,.

(B.109)

Scalar products are written as

a\cdot y\equiv a_{\mu}y^{\mu}=a^{+}y^{-}+a^{-}y^{+}-{a}_{\perp}\cdot{y}_{\perp}\,.

(B.110)

The light-cone derivatives are defined as follows

\partial^{+}=\frac{\partial}{\partial a^{-}}\,,\qquad\partial^{-}=\frac{\partial}{\partial a^{+}}\,,\qquad\partial^{\mu}_{\perp}=\frac{\partial}{\partial a_{\mu}^{\perp}}

(B.111)

Appendix C
Quark and Gluon GPDs from their Conformal (Gegenbauer) Moments

The framework for analysing the evolution equations for the GPDs, consists of defining the operators in the OPE in the
representations of the conformal group. The conformal operators transform
multiplicatively under renormalization. As a result, the anomalous dimensions of conformal operators are only
labeled by spin-j, in reference to the conformal spin of the composite operator
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
(and references therein).
In this section, we will briefly review the relevant quark and gluon spin-j operators, which enter the leading GPDs at twist-2.
The spin-j operators are the Gegenbauer moments of the various quark and gluon GPDs.

C.1
Quark and gluon operators

Quarks:

We define leading-twist bilocal quark operators as
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
:

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{qq}(w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\bar{\psi}(w^{-}_{1})[w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2}]\gamma^{+}\psi(w^{-}_{2})\,,

(C.112)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\cal O}^{qq}(w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\bar{\psi}(w^{-}_{1})[w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2}]\gamma^{+}\gamma^{5}\psi(w^{-}_{2})\,,

(C.113)

where have added the Wilson line in the adjoint representation

[w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2}]^{ab}=P\exp\left(g\int_{w^{-}_{2}}^{w^{-}_{1}}dw^{\prime-}f^{abc}A_{c}^{+}(w^{\prime-},{\bf 0}_{\perp})\right)\,,

to respect gauge invariance.

Gluons:

We define the two-dimensional tensors
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005

\displaystyle g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}\!\!\!

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!g_{\mu\nu}-n_{\mu}n^{\ast}_{\nu}-n_{\nu}n^{\ast}_{\mu}\,,

(C.114)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}\!\!\!

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!\varepsilon^{\alpha\beta\rho\sigma}g^{\perp}_{\alpha\mu}g^{\perp}_{\beta\nu}n^{\ast}_{\rho}n_{\sigma}\,,

(C.115)

where the totally antisymmetric tensor is normalized as
\varepsilon^{0123}=1
.

We also define the gauge potential in the light-cone gauge

A_{\perp}\equiv A^{x}+iA^{y}\,,\qquad\bar{A}_{\perp}\equiv A^{x}-iA^{y}\,,

(C.116)

and the gluon field-strength tensors

\displaystyle\partial^{+}A_{\perp}=F^{+x}-i\widetilde{F}^{+x}=i\left(F^{+y}-i\widetilde{F}^{+y}\right)\,,\qquad\partial^{+}\bar{A}_{\perp}=F^{+x}+i\widetilde{F}^{+x}=-i\left(F^{+y}+i\widetilde{F}^{+y}\right)\,,

F^{\ +\mu}_{[-]\,\perp}=F^{+\mu}_{\phantom{+}\,\perp}-i\widetilde{F}^{+\mu}_{\phantom{+}\,\perp}\,,\qquad F^{\ +\mu}_{[+]\,\perp}=F^{+\mu}_{\phantom{+}\,\perp}+i\widetilde{F}^{+\mu}_{\phantom{+}\,\perp}\,,

(C.117)

with the dual gluon field strength defined as
\widetilde{F}^{\mu\nu}\equiv 12\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}F_{\rho\sigma}
, so that
\widetilde{F}^{+}{}^{\mu}_{\perp}=\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}_{\perp}F^{+}{}^{\perp}_{\nu}
for two-dimensional transverse indices.

Using these definitions we introduce the leading-twist gluonic
operators
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
:

\displaystyle{\cal O}^{gg}(w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!F^{+\mu}_{a}(w^{-}_{1})[w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2}]^{ab}g_{\mu\nu}F^{\nu+}_{b}(w^{-}_{2})\,,

(C.118)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\cal O}^{gg}(w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!F^{+\mu}_{a}(w^{-}_{1})[w^{-}_{1},w^{-}_{2}]^{ab}i\varepsilon^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}F^{\nu+}_{b}(w^{-}_{2})\,,

(C.119)

C.2
Operator matrix elements and GPDs

We define the spinor bilinears
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005

\begin{array}[]{ll}b=\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,&\widetilde{b}=\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{5}u(p_{1})\,,\\
h^{\mu}=\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{\mu}u(p_{1})\,,&\widetilde{h}^{\mu}=\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}u(p_{1})\,,\\
t^{\mu\nu}=\bar{u}(p_{2})i\sigma^{\mu\nu}u(p_{1})\,,&\widetilde{t}^{\mu\nu}=\bar{u}(p_{2})i\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma^{5}u(p_{1})\,.\end{array}

(C.120)

The matrix elements for
quark operators can be decomposed in terms of the above spinor bilinears as

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}|{\cal O}^{qq}(-w^{-},w^{-})|p_{1}\rangle\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\int_{-1}^{1}dx\ {\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\left\{h^{+}H^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})+e^{+}E^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\right\}

(C.121)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!p^{+}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,{\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}F^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}|\widetilde{\cal O}^{qq}(-w^{-},w^{-})|p_{1}\rangle\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\int_{-1}^{1}dx\ {\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\left\{\tilde{h}^{+}\widetilde{H}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})+\tilde{e}^{+}\widetilde{E}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\right\}

(C.122)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!p^{+}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,{\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\widetilde{F}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\,.

Similarly, the matrix elements for
gluon operators can be decomposed in terms of the spinor bilinears as

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}|{\cal O}^{gg}(-w^{-},w^{-})|p_{1}\rangle\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{1}{4}p^{+}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\ {\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\left\{h^{+}H^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})+e^{+}E^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\right\}

(C.123)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{(p^{+})^{2}}{4}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,{\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}F^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\langle p_{2}|\widetilde{\cal O}^{gg}(-w^{-},w^{-})|p_{1}\rangle\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{1}{4}p^{+}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\ {\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\left\{\tilde{h}^{+}\widetilde{H}^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})+\tilde{e}^{+}\widetilde{E}^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\right\}

(C.124)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{(p^{+})^{2}}{4}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,{\rm e}^{-ixp^{+}w^{-}}\widetilde{F}^{g}(x,\eta,\Delta^{2})\,.

C.3
Spin-j quark and gluon operators

Classically, massless 4D QCD is invariant under the conformal group
SO(4,2)
with 15 generators (1-dilatation, 4-conformal, 4-translation, 6-Lorentz).
The OPE expansion on the light cone involves Wilsonian operators which transform covariantly under the Lorentz group
SO(3,1)
,
but not under the conformal group
SO(4,2)
. Since
SL(2)
on the light front, is a subgroup of the conformal group, a way
to re-organize the Wilsonian expansion is in terms of conformal tensors of spin
j=\frac{1}{2}(d+s)

(the eigenvalue of the collinear subgroup of the conformal operator) where the twist is
\tau=d-s

Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
.
More specifically,
the twist-2 vector quark and gluon operators are (see Eq.4.138 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\mathbb{O}^{qq}_{j}=\bar{\psi}(i\partial^{+})^{j-1}\gamma^{+}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}_{\leftrightarrow}}}{{\cal D}}{\!}^{+}\!/\partial^{+}\right)\psi\,,\qquad\mathbb{O}^{gg}_{j}=F^{+}{}_{\mu}(i\partial^{+})^{j-2}C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}_{\leftrightarrow}}}{{\cal D}}{\!}^{+}\!/\partial^{+}\right)F^{\mu+}\,,

(C.125)

and the twist-2 axial-vector quark operator is

\tilde{\mathbb{O}}^{qq}_{j}=\bar{\psi}(i\partial^{+})^{j-1}\gamma^{+}\gamma_{5}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}_{\leftrightarrow}}}{{\cal D}}{\!}^{+}\!/\partial^{+}\right)\psi\,,

(C.126)

Here
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{}_{\leftrightarrow}}}{{\cal D}}{\!}^{+}
is the symmetrized long derivative on LF
+
.
C_{n}^{\nu}(x)
are the Gegenbauer polynomials,
a generalization of the Legendre polynomials to
(2\nu+2)
-D space. Their explicit form in terms of hypergeometric functions is

\displaystyle C_{n}^{\nu}(x)=\begin{pmatrix}n+2\nu+1\\
n\end{pmatrix}\bigg(\frac{x+1}{2}\bigg)^{2}{}_{2}F_{1}\bigg(-n,-n-\nu+\frac{1}{2};\nu+\frac{1}{2};\frac{x-1}{x+1}\bigg)

(C.127)

with support in
[-1,+1]
, and the ortho-normalization with weight factor
w(x|\nu)=(1-x^{2})^{\nu-1/2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,(1-x^{2})^{\nu-\frac{1}{2}}\,C_{n}^{\nu}(x)C_{m}^{\nu}(x)=\frac{\pi 2^{1-2\nu}\Gamma(n+2\nu}{(n+\nu)\gamma^{2}(\nu)\Gamma(n+1)}\,\delta_{mn}

(C.128)

C.4
Conformal Moments of Quark and Gluon GPDs

Valence vector quark:

The conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of the valence vector quark GPDs, are directly related to the matrix elements
of the spin-j conformal operators in QCD (see Eq.4.243 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\eta^{j-1}\,\int_{0}^{1}dx\ C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)F_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=(p^{+})^{-(j-1)-1}\langle p_{2}|\mathbb{O}^{qq}_{j}(0)|p_{1}\rangle\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
. The valence and x-even combination

F_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})=F^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})+F^{q}(-x,\eta;\mu^{2})

stands for either
H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})
or
E_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})
.

Singlet vector quark:

The conformal (Gegenbauer) moments for the vector-singlet combinations, are also related to the spin-j conformal operators by (see Eq.4.243 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\eta^{j-1}\,\int_{0}^{1}dx\ C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)F_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=(p^{+})^{-(j-1)-1}\langle p_{2}|\mathbb{O}^{qq}_{j}(0)|p_{1}\rangle\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
. The valence and x-odd combination

F_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})=F^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})-F^{q}(-x,\eta;\mu^{2})

is either
H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})
or
E_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta;\mu^{2})
.

Singlet axial quark:

The conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of the singlet axial quark GPDs are related to the conformal operators in QCD via (straightforward generalization of Eq.4.243 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
for the axial GPDs)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\eta^{j-1}\,\int_{0}^{1}dx\ C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\widetilde{F}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=(p^{+})^{-(j-1)-1}\langle p_{2}|\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}^{qq}_{j}(0)|p_{1}\rangle\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
. Again, the x-even singlet combination

\widetilde{F}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\widetilde{F}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})+\widetilde{F}^{q}(-x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})

will refer to either
\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
or
\widetilde{E}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
.

Valence axial quark:

The conformal (Gegenbauer) moments for the axial-valence combinations, are tied to the spin-j twist-2 axial operator
(straightforward generalization of Eq.4.243 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
for the axial GPDs)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\eta^{j-1}\,\int_{0}^{1}dx\ C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\widetilde{F}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=(p^{+})^{-(j-1)-1}\langle p_{2}|\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}^{qq}_{j}(0)|p_{1}\rangle\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
. The x-odd valence axial combination

\widetilde{F}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\widetilde{F}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})-\widetilde{F}^{q}(-x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})

will refer to either
\widetilde{H}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
or
\widetilde{E}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
.

Gluons:

For gluon operators, we have (see Eq.4.284 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=2\eta^{j-2}\,\int_{0}^{1}dx\ C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=4(p^{+})^{-(j-1)-1}\langle p_{2}|\mathbb{O}^{gg}_{j}(0)|p_{1}\rangle\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
.

C.5
Reconstruction of Quark and Gluon GPDs from their Conformal Moments

The conformal (Gegenbauer) moments can be inverted using their orthonormality (
C.128
), to give the quark and gluon GPDs at a given resolution in explicit form.
We now give the inversion with explicit support in
|x|\leq\eta
, and briefly comment on the inversion in the full support
|x|\leq 1
.

Valence vector quark:

The valence vector quark GPD, follows by inversion(see Eq.4.242 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
, with

\displaystyle N_{j}(\nu)=2^{1-2\nu}\frac{{\Gamma}^{2}(1/2){\Gamma}(2\nu+j)}{{\Gamma}^{2}(\nu){\Gamma}(j+1)(\nu+j)}

(C.135)

Singlet vector quark:

Similarly, the singlet vector quark GPD, is (see Eq.4.264 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

(C.136)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

for even
j=2,4,...
.

Valence axial quark:

The valence axial quark GPD, is

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

(C.137)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

for even
j=2,4,...
.

Singlet axial quark:

The singlet axial quark GPD, is

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2}

(C.138)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
.

Gluons:

The gluon GPD is (see Eq.4.264 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{5}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-2}\,N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-2}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}\times C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
.

Appendix D
RG evolution of quark and gluon GPDs in QCD

D.1
RG evolution of non-singlet (valence) quark GPDs

The leading-order RG evolution of Gegenbauer moments of valence (non-singlet) vector quark GPDs is given by (see Eq.4.244 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma_{(0)j-1}^{qq;\text{V};\text{NS}}/\beta_{0}}

(D.140)

for odd
j=1,3,...
, where the input valence quark GPDs
\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
are given in (
II.10
), and the vector non-singlet anomalous dimensions are

\displaystyle\gamma^{qq;\text{V}}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{F}\left(-4\psi(j+2)+4\psi(1)+\frac{2}{(j+1)(j+2)}+3\right)\,.

(D.141)

The Euler
\psi
-function is given
in terms of the harmonic numbers

\psi(n)=\frac{d}{dn}\ln{\Gamma}(n)=\psi(1)+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{k}\,.

(D.142)

with the Euler constant
\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle\rm E}=-\psi(1)\approx 0.577216
.

The leading and one-loop RG evolution equation for the valence (non-singlet) vector quark GPDs is given by (see Eq.4.240 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\frac{d}{d\ln\mu}H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=-\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{dy}{\eta}k^{qq}_{(0)}\left(\frac{x}{\eta},\frac{y}{\eta}\right)H_{valence}^{q}(y,\eta,t;\mu^{2})+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,,

(D.143)

k^{qq}_{(0)}\left({x}/{\eta},{y}/{\eta}\right)
is the leading-order quark-quark generalized exclusive kernel
(see Eq.4.38 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
for its explicit form), with Gegenbauer polynomials
C_{j-1}^{3/2}(x)

as eigenfunctions,

\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)k^{qq}_{(0)}\left(\frac{x}{\eta},\frac{y}{\eta}\right)=\gamma_{(0)j-1}^{qq;\text{V}}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{y}{\eta}\right)\,.

(D.144)

Conversely, since the exclusive kernel is diagonal in the Gegenbauer basis which is complete in
L^{2}[-1,1]
,
the explicit solution to (
D.143
) is (see Eq.4.242 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

(D.145)

for odd
j=1,3,...
. Here the weight and normalization factors are

w(x|\nu)=(1-x^{2})^{\nu-1/2}\,,\qquad N_{j-1}(\nu)=2^{1-2\nu}\frac{{\Gamma}^{2}(1/2){\Gamma}(2\nu+(j-1))}{{\Gamma}^{2}(\nu){\Gamma}((j-1)+1)(\nu+(j-1))}\,.

D.2
RG evolution of singlet quark and gluon GPDs

The combined singlet vector quark and gluon GPDs

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\equiv\left({\sum_{q}F_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\atop F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})}\right)\,,

(D.146)

evolve in leading order as (see Eq.4.264 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\mathbf{F}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}{\bf E}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

(D.147)

for even
j=2,4,...
, with the matrix valued coeffcients

{\mbox{\bf E}}_{j}(x)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}E_{j-1}(x|\frac{3}{2})&0\\
0&E_{j-2}(x|\frac{5}{2})\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\qquad E_{j}(x|\nu)=\frac{w\left(x|\nu\right)}{\eta^{j}N_{j}(\nu)}C_{j}^{\nu}(x)\,.

(D.148)

Note that we do not need to re-expand the GPDs using additional orthogonal polynomials, since our input GPDs are non-zero only for
-\eta<x<\eta
.
(
D.147
) is invertible

\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})=\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\eta^{j-1}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(x/\eta\right)&0\\
0&\eta^{j-2}\,C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(x/\eta\right)\end{array}\right)\,\mathbf{F}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

(D.149)

thanks to the ortogonality and completeness of the Gegenbauer polynomials.

(
D.149
) evolves as (see Eq.4.271 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})={\mathbf{\cal E}}_{j-1}(\mu^{2},\mu_{0}^{2})\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2}_{0})\,,

(D.150)

with the evolution operator

\displaystyle\mathbf{\cal E}_{j}\left(\mu^{2},\mu_{0}^{2}\right)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!{P}^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j}/\beta_{0}}+{P}^{-}_{j}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j}/\beta_{0}}\,,

(D.151)

given in terms of the projection operators

{P}^{\pm}_{j}=\frac{\pm 1}{\gamma^{+}_{j}-\gamma^{-}_{j}}\left({\gamma}_{(0)j}-\gamma^{\mp}_{j}\,\right)\,,

(D.152)

constructed from the eigenvalues of the leading-order anomalous dimension matrix

{\gamma}_{(0)j}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j}&\gamma^{qg}_{(0)j}\\
\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j}&\gamma^{gg}_{(0)j}\end{array}\right)\,,

(D.153)

as

\gamma^{\pm}_{j}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j}+\gamma^{gg}_{(0)j}\pm\sqrt{\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j}-\gamma^{gg}_{(0)j}\right)^{2}+4\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j}\gamma^{qg}_{(0)j}}\right)\,.

(D.154)

The projection operators have the properties

({P}^{\pm}_{j})^{2}={P}^{\pm}_{j}\,,\qquad{P}^{+}_{j}{P}^{-}_{j}=0\,,\qquad{P}^{+}_{j}+{P}^{-}_{j}={1}\,,

(D.155)

and

{\gamma}_{(0)j}=\gamma_{j}^{+}{P}^{+}_{j}+\gamma_{j}^{-}{P}^{-}_{j}\,.

(D.156)

Note that, at leading-order, the evolution operator satisfies the
equation (see Eq.4.272 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\frac{d}{d\ln\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\mathbf{\cal E}_{j}\left(\mu^{2},\mu_{0}^{2}\right)=-\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}{\gamma}_{(0)j}\mathbf{\cal E}_{j}\left(\mu^{2},\mu_{0}^{2}\right)\,,

(D.157)

with the boundary condition

\mathbf{\cal E}_{j}\left(\mu_{0}^{2},\mu_{0}^{2}\right)={1}=\left({1\ \,0\atop 0\ \,1}\right)\,.

In addition, the leading-order diagonal anomalous dimensions are given by (see Eq.4.152-4.159 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle\gamma^{qq;V}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{F}\left(-4\psi(j+2)+4\psi(1)+\frac{2}{(j+1)(j+2)}+3\right)\,,

(D.158)

\displaystyle\gamma^{qg;V}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-24N_{f}T_{F}\frac{j^{2}+3j+4}{j(j+1)(j+2)(j+3)}\,,

(D.159)

\displaystyle\gamma^{gq;V}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{F}\frac{j^{2}+3j+4}{3(j+1)(j+2)}\,,

(D.160)

\displaystyle\gamma^{gg;V}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{A}\left(-4\psi(j+2)+4\psi(1)+8\frac{j^{2}+3j+3}{j(j+1)(j+2)(j+3)}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{C_{A}}\right)\,,

(D.161)

and

\displaystyle\gamma^{qq;A}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{F}\left(-4\psi(j+2)+4\psi(1)+\frac{2}{(j+1)(j+2)}+3\right)\,,

(D.162)

\displaystyle\gamma^{qg;A}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-24N_{f}T_{F}\frac{1}{(j+1)(j+2)}\,,

(D.163)

\displaystyle\gamma^{gq;A}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{F}\frac{j(j+3)}{3(j+1)(j+2)}\,,

(D.164)

\displaystyle\gamma^{gg;A}_{(0)j}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-C_{A}\left(-4\psi(j+2)+4\psi(1)+\frac{8}{(j+1)(j+2)}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{C_{A}}\right)\,.

(D.165)

We can rewrite the RG evolution of the singlet Gegenbauer moments (
D.150
) more explicitly in terms of the projection operators as

\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})={P}^{+}_{j-1}\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}+{P}^{-}_{j-1}\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,.

(D.166)

And, using

{P}^{\pm}_{j-1}{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\frac{\pm 2}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{\mp}_{j-1}\\
\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\end{array}\right)F^{\pm}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

(D.167)

with

F^{\pm}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})=\frac{1}{4}\frac{\gamma^{qg}_{(0)j-1}}{\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{\mp}_{j-1}}\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

(D.168)

we find the the evolved singlet quark, and gluon GPDs in matrix form as

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\\
\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\end{array}\right)F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{-2}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\\
\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\end{array}\right)F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\\
\frac{2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\end{array}\right)F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\\
\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\end{array}\right)F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\\
\frac{2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\\
\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}+\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\\
\frac{2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}+\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\end{array}\right)\,.

Therefore, we explicitly find

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\\
\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\end{array}\right)\,,

where

\displaystyle\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

(D.192)

\displaystyle\propto

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

(D.193)

\displaystyle\propto

\displaystyle\frac{2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times F^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times F^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
. Note that we fix the proportionality constants in the above equations (
D.192
) and (
D.193
) by demanding that
\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2}=\mu_{0}^{2})=0
, and
\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2}=\mu_{0}^{2})=\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

(D.194)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\times\frac{2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times\mathbb{F}^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\times\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{qq}_{(0)j-1}-\gamma^{+}_{j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times\mathbb{F}^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})

(D.195)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\times\frac{2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times\mathbb{F}^{+}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\times\frac{-2\left(\gamma^{gq}_{(0)j-1}\right)}{\gamma^{+}_{j-1}-\gamma^{-}_{j-1}}\times\mathbb{F}^{-}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2}_{0})}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}\right)^{\gamma^{-}_{j-1}/\beta_{0}}\,.

Finally, in terms of the
evolved conformal (Gegenbauer) moments (
III.21
-
III.22
), the evolved singlet quark and gluon GPDs are given by

\displaystyle\sum_{q}F_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\left(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\right)}{\eta^{j-1}N_{j-1}\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\sum_{q}\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

and

\displaystyle F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\left(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{5}{2}\right)}{\eta^{j-2}N_{j-2}\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)}C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,,

(D.197)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-2}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-2}\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)}\times C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,.

D.3
Evolution of quark and gluon GPDs to the asymptotic regime
\mu\rightarrow\infty

For completeness,
we summarise the asymptotic forms of the GPDs at
infinite resolution, which are fixed by the free parton model, and are dominated by the leading spin-1 or spin-2 Gegenbauer moments.
More explicitly, the asymptotic GPDs for non-singlet (valence) quark GPDs, are (see Eq.4.245 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times F^{q}_{1}(t)\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

\displaystyle E_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times F^{q}_{2}(t)\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,.

For singlet quark GPDs, they are (see Eq.4.289 and Eq.4.290 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle\sum_{q}H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{15}{4}\frac{N_{f}}{4C_{F}+N_{f}}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\frac{x}{\eta}\times\sum_{q}\bigg\{A_{q}(t)+\eta^{2}D_{q}(t)\bigg\}\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{q}E_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{15}{4}\frac{N_{f}}{4C_{F}+N_{f}}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\frac{x}{\eta}\times\sum_{q}\bigg\{E_{q}(t)-\eta^{2}D_{q}(t)\bigg\}\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,.

And, for gluon GPDs, they are (see Eq.4.291 and Eq.4.292 in
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
)

\displaystyle H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{15}{8}\frac{4C_{F}}{4C_{F}+N_{f}}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\bigg\{A_{g}(t)+\eta^{2}D_{g}(t)\bigg\}\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

\displaystyle E^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2}\to\infty)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{15}{8}\frac{4C_{F}}{4C_{F}+N_{f}}\times\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\bigg\{E_{g}(t)-\eta^{2}D_{g}(t)\bigg\}\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,.

D.4
Matching
\lambda
in soft-wall holographic QCD to
\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})
in pQCD

In our previous work
Mamo and Zahed 2020
, we have fixed the
′
t Hooft coupling
\lambda=11.243
in the soft-wall holographic QCD,
by using the photoproduction data for
J/\Psi
. Now, we suggest to map this fixed and large
′
t Hooft coupling in holographic QCD, to the running

′
t Hooft coupling in pQCD, which will allow us to determine the value of the initial scale
\mu_{0}
. More specifically, the running

′
t Hooft coupling
\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})=g_{s}^{2}(\mu_{0}^{2})N_{c}=\alpha_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})\times 4\pi\times N_{c}
at
\mu=\mu_{0}
, maps onto

\displaystyle\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})=\lambda=11.243\,.

(D.201)

which translates to

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})=\frac{\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})}{4\pi N_{c}}=\frac{\lambda}{4\pi N_{c}}=\frac{11.243}{4\pi N_{c}}=0.298\,.

(D.202)

This result is remarkably close to the value of
\frac{1}{3}
commonly used to initialize pQCD evolutions.
This is welcome, since our holographic description is set to describe the non-perturbative physics at

\mu_{0}
. Therefore, using
\alpha_{s}(\mu_{0}^{2})=0.298
in
the two-loop exact transcendental
equation, for the QCD running coupling constant
\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})
,
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005

-\beta_{0}\ln\frac{\mu^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}_{\overline{\rm MS}}}=\frac{4\pi}{\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{0}}\ln\left(-\frac{4\pi}{\beta_{0}\alpha_{s}(\mu^{2})}-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{0}^{2}}\right)\,,

(D.203)

with

\displaystyle\beta_{0}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{4}{3}T_{F}N_{f}-\frac{11}{3}C_{A}\,,

(D.204)

\displaystyle\beta_{1}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{10}{3}C_{A}N_{f}+2C_{F}N_{f}-\frac{34}{3}C_{A}^{2}\,,

(D.205)

and

T_{F}=\frac{1}{2}\,,\qquad C_{F}=\frac{N_{c}^{2}-1}{2N_{c}}\,,\qquad C_{A}=N_{c}\ ,

(D.206)

we find the input scale
\mu_{0}=1.808~\text{GeV}
(or
\mu_{0}^{2}=3.269~\text{GeV}^{2}
), using
N_{c}=N_{f}=3
, and
\Lambda_{\overline{\rm MS}}=0.34~\text{GeV}
.

Appendix E
Exclusive electroproduction of mesons and photon with twist-2 quark and gluon GPDs in QCD

In this section of the appendix, we review the various amplitudes for electroproduction of mesons and photon with twist-2 quark and gluon GPDs in QCD.

E.1
Electroproduction of vector mesons with twist-2 gluon GPD in QCD

In pQCD, the twist-2 gluon GPD drives the near-forward electromagnetic production of heavy vector mesons such as
charmonia and bottomonia, in the Regge limit, as illustrated in Fig.
1
(left).
More specifically, the gluon GPDs contribution
F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
to the longitudinal
vector meson electroproduction amplitude is given by (see Eq.282 in
Diehl 2003
)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{L\nu}^{\prime*}T_{t(\text{with gluon GPDs})}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm gluon}^{LL}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{8}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\times g_{s}^{2}(\mu^{2})N_{c}\times\frac{1}{Q}

\displaystyle\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\sum_{q}e_{q}\frac{\Phi^{q}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\frac{F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})}{x}\left[\frac{1}{\xi-x-i\epsilon}-\frac{1}{\xi+x-i\epsilon}\right]

In leading order in
1/Q
and
\alpha_{s}
, the meson structure function enters as a constant,
through the integral of the pertinent quark flavor combination in the meson DA
\Phi^{q}(z)
.
On the light front,

h^{+}=\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{+}u(p_{1})\approx\frac{p^{+}}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1}),

and ignoring the Pauli contribution
E^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
to
F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
for
Q\gg\sqrt{-t}
, we have

\displaystyle F^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\approx\frac{h^{+}}{p^{+}}H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})=H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

(E.208)

which simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm gluon}^{LL}(\xi,t,\mu^{2})=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu^{2})

\displaystyle\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\sum_{q}e_{q}\frac{\Phi^{q}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\times\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\frac{H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})}{(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}})}\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where we have defined the running ’t Hooft coupling constant in QCD as
\lambda_{s}(\mu^{2})\equiv g_{s}^{2}(\mu^{2})N_{c}
.

The gluon GPD at
\mu=\mu_{0}
can be written in terms of their Gegenbauer (conformal) moments
\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j-2}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
as

\displaystyle H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{5}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-2}\,N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-2}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}\times C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
, where we used the weight and normalization factors as

w(x|\nu)=(1-x^{2})^{\nu-1/2}\,,\qquad N_{j-2}(\nu)=2^{1-2\nu}\frac{{\Gamma}^{2}(1/2){\Gamma}(2\nu+(j-2))}{{\Gamma}^{2}(\nu){\Gamma}((j-2)+1)(\nu+(j-2))}\,.

And, using the conformal expansion of gluon GPDs (
E.1
) in the amplitude (
E.1
), we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm gluon}^{LL}(\xi,t,\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu^{2})\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\sum_{q}e_{q}\frac{\Phi^{q}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\Bigg[\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\times\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\frac{H^{g}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})}{(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}})}

(E.211)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi H^{g}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi H^{g}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu^{2})\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\sum_{q}e_{q}\frac{\Phi^{q}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-2}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,\frac{\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{2}}{(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}})}\times C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi H^{g}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi H^{g}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu^{2})\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\sum_{q}e_{q}\frac{\Phi^{q}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-2}(\frac{5}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\,\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\right)\times C_{j-2}^{5/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

where in the last line we have used
\eta\sim\xi
, and dropped the
i\pi H^{g}
contributions as they vanish in the Regge kinematics.

E.2
DVCS with twist-2 quark GPDs in QCD

The leading order pQCD deep virtual Compton scattering (DVCS) amplitude written in terms of quark GPDs in the twist-2 approximation, is given by
Belitsky and Radyushkin 2005
(see Eq. 5.32 there)

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}_{\text{with quark GPDs}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{1}{2p\cdot\tilde{q}}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}C^{q[-]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)\left\{(p\cdot\tilde{q})S^{\mu\nu;\rho\sigma}n_{\sigma}F^{q}_{\rho}(x,\eta,\Delta)+i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}p_{\sigma}\left(x\,\widetilde{F}^{q}_{\rho}(x,\eta,\Delta)\right)\right\}

(E.212)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{1}{2p\cdot\tilde{q}}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}C^{q[+]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)\left\{2i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}\widetilde{F}^{q}_{\rho}(x,\eta,\Delta)\right\}\,.

The tree-level coefficient
functions are

C^{q[\pm]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)=\frac{Q_{q}^{2}}{\xi-x-i\epsilon}\pm\frac{Q_{q}^{2}}{\xi+x-i\epsilon}\equiv Q_{q}^{2}\times C^{[\pm]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)\,,

(E.213)

and the distributions in the twist-2 approximation are given by

\displaystyle F^{q}_{\rho}(x,\eta,\Delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!p_{\rho}\left(\frac{h^{+}}{p^{+}}H^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)+\frac{e^{+}}{p^{+}}E^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\right)=p_{\rho}\times F^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\,,

(E.214)

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}^{q}_{\rho}(x,\eta,\Delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!p_{\rho}\left(\frac{\widetilde{h}^{+}}{p^{+}}\widetilde{H}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)+\frac{\widetilde{e}^{+}}{p^{+}}\widetilde{E}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\right)=p_{\rho}\times\widetilde{F}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\,.

(E.215)

Vector contribution:

By contracting (
E.212
) with the transverse
TT
polarization tensors, we can separate the vector contribution to DVCS as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{T\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}T^{\mu\nu}_{\text{with quark GPDs}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon_{T}^{\prime}\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}C^{q[-]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)\times p^{+}\times F^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\,.

(E.216)

The singlet vector quark GPDs at
\mu=\mu_{0}
can be written in terms of their conformal (Gegenbauer) moments
\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
as

\displaystyle H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
, where we used the weight and normalization factors as

w(x|\nu)=(1-x^{2})^{\nu-1/2}\,,\qquad N_{j-1}(\nu)=2^{1-2\nu}\frac{{\Gamma}^{2}(1/2){\Gamma}(2\nu+(j-1))}{{\Gamma}^{2}(\nu){\Gamma}((j-1)+1)(\nu+(j-1))}\,.

And, using the conformal expansion of singlet quark GPDs (
E.2
) in the vector part of the amplitude (
E.216
), we find

\displaystyle\epsilon_{T\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}T^{\mu\nu}_{\text{with quark GPDs}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon_{T}^{\prime}\times N_{c}^{0}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times\frac{2x}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\times H_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times H_{singlet}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times H_{singlet}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!-\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon_{T}^{\prime}\times N_{c}^{0}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\xi}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times 2\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,\frac{\frac{x}{\xi}\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)}{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times H_{singlet}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times H_{singlet}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\!\!\!-\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon_{T}^{\prime}\times N_{c}^{0}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\times\frac{x}{\xi}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
, and, in the last line, we have used
\eta\sim\xi
. The
i\pi H^{q}
contribution vanishes at
\xi=\pm\eta
.

Axial contribution:

By contracting (
E.212
) with the longitudinal-transverse
LT
polarization tensors, we can separate the axial contribution to DVCS as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}T^{\mu\nu}_{\text{with quark GPDs}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p\cdot\tilde{q}}\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}C^{q[+]}_{(0)}(x,\xi)\left\{\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}\times 2i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}p_{\rho}\times\widetilde{F}^{q}(x,\eta,\Delta)\right\}\,.

(E.219)

The singlet axial-vectror quark GPDs at
\mu=\mu_{0}
can be written in terms of their Gegenbauer (conformal) moments
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
as

\displaystyle\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\tilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

for odd
j=1,3,...
. And, using the conformal expansion of the axial-vector singlet quark GPDs (
E.2
) in the axial part of the DVCS amplitude (
E.219
), we find

\displaystyle\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}T^{\mu\nu}_{\text{with quark GPDs}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}\times N_{c}^{0}\times i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}p_{\rho}\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{2}{Q^{2}}\times\xi\times\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\frac{2\xi}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}\times N_{c}^{0}\times i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}p_{\rho}\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{2}{Q^{2}}\times\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{2}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,\frac{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}}{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\widetilde{H}_{singlet}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\!\!\!\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}\times N_{c}^{0}\times i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}p_{\rho}\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{4}{Q^{2}}\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{\xi^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}^{2}\,\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(singlet)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
. In the last line we have used
\eta\sim\xi
, and dropped the
i\pi\widetilde{H}^{q}
in the Regge limit.

E.3
Pair meson electroproduction with non-singlet (valence) vector quark GPDs in QCD

In leading order in
\alpha_{s}
, the
amplitude for electroproduction of pair of pions (or any other pair of mesons) in pQCD
is illustrated in Fig.
2
left.
It is given in terms of gluon DA of mesons, and quark GPDs as (see Eq.283 in
Diehl 2003
, and Eq.10 in
Lehmann-Dronke
et al. 2001
)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi\pi p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L};m_{\pi\pi})=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{2\times 4\pi\alpha_{s}}{N_{c}}\frac{1}{Q}\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\frac{\Phi^{g}(z)}{z(1-z)}\,

\displaystyle\times\int_{-1}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,F^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\left[\frac{1}{\xi-x-i\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\xi+x-i\epsilon}\right]\,.

In leading order,
\Phi^{g}(z)
is gluon distribution amplitude for the emission of 2 pions, with
z
a gluon-parton fraction of the
2 pion longitudinal momentum in the final state.
F^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})
is the unpolarized and skewed quark parton distribution
in the nucleon, with both skewed Dirac and Pauli form factors. In the Regge limit with
s\gg-t
, we can ignore
the Pauli contribution and rewrite (
E.3
) as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi\pi p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L};m_{\pi\pi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{2g_{s}^{2}N_{c}}{N_{c}^{2}}\frac{1}{Q}\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\frac{\Phi^{g}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\left[\frac{1}{\xi-x-i\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\xi+x-i\epsilon}\right]\,.

The non-singlet (valence) vector quark GPDs at
\mu=\mu_{0}
can be written in terms of their conformal (Gegenbauer) moments
\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
as

\displaystyle H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\times\theta(\eta-|x|)\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
, where we have used the weight and normalization factors as

w(x|\nu)=(1-x^{2})^{\nu-1/2}\,,\qquad N_{j-1}(\nu)=2^{1-2\nu}\frac{{\Gamma}^{2}(1/2){\Gamma}(2\nu+(j-1))}{{\Gamma}^{2}(\nu){\Gamma}((j-1)+1)(\nu+(j-1))}\,.

And, using the conformal expansion (
E.3
) in the amplitude (
E.3
), we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi\pi p}(s,t,Q_{0},\epsilon_{L};m_{\pi\pi})=e\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times\frac{2}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\frac{\Phi^{g}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\frac{2\xi}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times\frac{2}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\frac{\Phi^{g}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\frac{2}{\xi}\times\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\times\frac{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}}{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})+i\pi\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,H_{valence}^{q}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle e\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times\frac{4}{N_{c}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{Q_{0}}\times\left[\int_{0}^{1}dz\,\frac{\Phi^{g}(z)}{z(1-z)}\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]\times\sum_{q}Q_{q}\,\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
. In the last line, we have used
\eta\sim\xi
, and dropped the
i\pi H^{q}
contributions as they
vanish in the Regge limit.

E.4
Neutral pion electroproduction with non-singlet (valence) axial quark GPDs in QCD

In the leading twist-expansion, the pQCD electroproduction of neutral pion (
\pi^{0}
) in Fig.
4
left, can be written
in terms of the meson DAs and the nucleon axial quark GPDs (see Eq.230 with Eq.231 and Eq.232 in
Goeke et al. 2001
)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle e\times{C_{F}\over N_{c}}\times{1\over 2}\times(4\pi\alpha_{s})\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\pi}(z)}\over z}\right]

(E.226)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{+}}\left\{A_{\pi^{0}N}\,\widetilde{h}^{+}\,+\,B_{\pi^{0}N}\,\widetilde{e}^{+}\right\}\,,

where
\Phi_{\pi^{0}}(z)
is the pion distribution amplitude (DA),

\displaystyle A_{\pi^{0}\,p}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{-1}^{1}dx\;{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}\,{\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{{{1}\over{\xi-x-i\epsilon}}-{{1}\over{\xi+x-i\epsilon}}\right\}

\displaystyle B_{\pi^{0}\,p}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\int_{-1}^{1}dx\;{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}\,{\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{E}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{E}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{{{1}\over{\xi-x-i\epsilon}}-{{1}\over{\xi+x-i\epsilon}}\right\}\,.

(E.227)

Using the Casimir
C_{F}=\frac{N_{c}^{2}-1}{2N_{c}}\approx\frac{N_{c}}{2}
in the large-
N_{c}
limit, and defining the ’t Hooft coupling constant as
g_{s}^{2}N_{c}=\lambda_{s}
, we can re-write (
E.226
) as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\,=\,e\times{1\over 2}\times{1\over 2}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}}\times\lambda_{s}\times{1\over{Q}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\pi}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times u(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\,\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})\times{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}dx\;{\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu^{2})\right)}\;\left\{{{1}\over{\xi-x-i\epsilon}}-{{1}\over{\xi+x-i\epsilon}}\right\}\,,

where we have ignored the Pauli contribution for
s\gg-t
.

The non-singlet (valence) axial quark GPDs at
\mu=\mu_{0}
can be written in terms of their Gegenbauer (conformal) moments
\tilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{valence}^{q}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{w\big(\frac{x}{\eta}|\frac{3}{2}\big)}{\eta^{j-1}\,N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta}\times\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\times\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
. And, using the conformal expansion of axial-vector valence quark GPDs (
E.4
) in the
\pi^{0}
production amplitude (
E.4
), we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi^{0}p}(s,t,Q_{0},\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times{1\over 2}\times{1\over 2}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times{1\over{Q_{0}}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\pi}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times u(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\,\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})\times{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\frac{2x}{\xi^{2}-x^{2}}\times\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(x,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle e\times{1\over 2}\times{1\over 2}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times{1\over{Q_{0}}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\pi}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times u(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\,\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})\times{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[\frac{2}{\xi}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\eta^{j-1}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\frac{dx}{\eta}\,\frac{\frac{x}{\xi}\left(1-\frac{x^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)}{1-\frac{x^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\times C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\eta}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\pi\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{u}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{H}_{valence}^{d}(-\xi,\eta,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\,\approx\,
