\displaystyle e\times{1\over 2}\times\frac{1}{N_{c}}\times\lambda_{s}(\mu_{0})\times{1\over{Q_{0}}}\times\left[\,\int_{0}^{1}dz{{\Phi_{\pi}(z)}\over z}\right]\times\frac{1}{p^{+}}\times u(p_{2})\gamma^{+}\,\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})\times{-1\over{\sqrt{2}}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{N_{j-1}(\frac{3}{2})}\times\left[\int_{0}^{\xi}\frac{dx}{\xi}\,\frac{x}{\xi}\,C_{j-1}^{3/2}\left(\frac{x}{\xi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(Q_{u}\ \widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\,-\,Q_{d}\ \widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\eta\sim\xi,t;\mu_{0}^{2})\right)\,,

for even
j=2,4,...
. Again, in the last line, we have used
\eta\sim\xi
, and dropped the
i\pi\widetilde{H}
contributions.

Appendix F
Details on the resummation by the j-contour for gluon GPD contribution in the electroproduction of
\rho^{0}

Resummation by j-contour

We first rewrite the sum over even
j=2,4,...
in (
IV.2
) as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{dj}{4i}\frac{1+e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}\times\mathcal{N}_{g}(j)\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

(F.231)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e\times f_{V}^{\prime}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where the contour
\mathbb{C}
is at the right most of the branch point of

\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}=\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\sqrt{2}\lambda^{1/4}(j-j_{0g})^{1/2}\right)}

, and leftmost of the poles at even
j=2,4,...
. Here we have defined

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})=\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)\times\mathbb{F}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

(F.232)

to make explicit the branch point at
j=j_{0g}=2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}
. originating from the holographic input conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of gluon GPD at
\mu=\mu_{0}\sim Q_{0}
.

We now evaluate the input gluon GPD part of the evolved gluon GPD

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{dj}{4i}\frac{1+e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}\times\mathcal{N}_{g}(j)\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

(F.233)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e\times f_{V}^{\prime}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,.

by wrapping the j-plane contour
\mathbb{C}
to the left

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0}}\frac{dj}{2i}\frac{1+e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{Im}\left[\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\right]\times\mathcal{N}_{g}(j)\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

(F.234)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e\times f_{V}^{\prime}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where we have defined

iy=i\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0g}-j)}\,.

For,
y\rightarrow 0
, we may approximate
\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\approx iy\,e^{i\gamma_{E}y}
with the Euler constant
\gamma_{E}\approx 0.577216
, and write
\text{Im}\left[\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\right]\approx y\,\text{cos}(\gamma_{E}y)\approx\text{sin}(y)
for
y\rightarrow 0
or
j\rightarrow j_{0g}
. Therefore, for
j\rightarrow j_{0g}
, we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

(F.235)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle-\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0g}}\frac{dj}{2}\frac{1+e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0g}-j)}\right)\,.

Real and Imaginary parts

We separate the amplitude (
F.235
) into its real and imaginary parts

{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\equiv{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}=\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]+i\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]

,

\displaystyle\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle-\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0g}}\frac{dj}{2}\frac{1+\text{cos}\pi j}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0g}-j)}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

(F.236)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0g}}\frac{dj}{2}\,\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0g}-j)}\right)\,.

With the change of integration variable
y^{2}=2\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0g}-j)
, we turn the imaginary part into a Gaussian integral that can be evaluated exactly (with
\tilde{\tau}=\text{log}(1/\xi)
)

\displaystyle\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

(F.237)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0g}}}\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dy}{4\pi i\sqrt{\lambda}}\,ye^{-\tilde{\tau}y^{2}/2\sqrt{\lambda}}e^{iy}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q}(j_{0g})\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0g}}}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-2\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\,,

We compute the real part of the amplitude
\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]
by first expanding its prefactor near
j=2

\displaystyle\frac{1+\text{cos}\pi j}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\simeq\frac{2}{\pi(j-2)}+\mathcal{O}(j-2)\,,

(F.238)

and establishing the identity
\partial_{\tilde{\tau}}\left[e^{-2\tilde{\tau}}\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]\right]=-(2/\pi)e^{-2\tilde{\tau}}\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]
which gives us an approximation for the real part

\displaystyle\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\times\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\times\int_{\tilde{\tau}}^{\infty}d\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}\,\frac{2e^{-2\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda}-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}}}{\pi\tilde{\tau}^{\prime 3/2}}\,.

Small corrections in the order of
\mathcal{O}(\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]/\text{log}(1/\xi))
and
\mathcal{O}(\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]/\sqrt{\lambda})
to (
F
), can be computed in a standard perturbation series but can be ignored for
\xi\rightarrow 0
and fixed large
\sqrt{\lambda}
.

Small-
\xi
regime

We then compute the integral in (
F
) in the small-
\xi
regime, i.e.,
\tilde{\tau}=\text{log}(1/\xi)\rightarrow\infty
at fixed large
\sqrt{\lambda}
where the integral is dominated by its end point, and can be approximated by

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{\tau}}^{\infty}d\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}\,\frac{2e^{-2\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda}-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}}}{\pi\tilde{\tau}^{\prime 3/2}}=(\sqrt{\lambda}/\pi)\times e^{-2\tilde{\tau}/\sqrt{\lambda}}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\lambda}/\tilde{\tau})\right)\,.

(F.240)

Finally, combining this approximation for
\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]
with the exact result for
\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}\right]
(
F.237
), we find the full complex amplitude with evolved gluon GPD (for input gluon GPD part)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(gluon)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

(F.241)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Bigg[(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/\pi)\times e^{-2\tilde{\tau}/\sqrt{\lambda}}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle i\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0g}}}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{g}(j_{0g})\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{g}_{j_{0g}}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\times e\,f_{V}^{\prime}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\xi^{2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}}}\times\left[(\sqrt{\lambda}/\pi)+i\right]\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/2\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\,,

with
j_{0g}=2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}
, and
\tilde{\tau}=\text{log}(1/\xi)
.

Appendix G
Details on the resummation by the j-contour for valence quark GPDs contribution in the electroproduction of
\rho^{+}

Resummation by j-contour

We rewrite the sum over odd
j=1,3,...
in (
VI
) as contour integrals in the complex j-plane

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\approx\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{dj}{4i}\frac{1-e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}\times\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\times(-1)\times e\times f_{V}^{+}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where the contour
\mathbb{C}
is at the right most of the branch point of

\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}=\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\lambda^{1/4}(j-j_{0q})^{1/2}\right)}

and leftmost of the poles at odd
j=1,3,...
. Here we have defined

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})=\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)\times\mathbb{F}^{q}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})

(G.243)

in order to reveal the branch point at
j=j_{0q}=1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}
coming from our holographic input conformal (Gegenbauer) moments of valence quark GPDs at
\mu=\mu_{0}\sim Q_{0}
.

We evaluate the amplitude (
G
) by wrapping the j-plane contour
\mathbb{C}
to the left

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0q}}\frac{dj}{2i}\frac{1-e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{Im}\left[\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j)\times(-1)\times e\times f_{V}^{+}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where we have defined

iy=i\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0q}-j)}\,.

For,
y\rightarrow 0
, we may approximate
\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\approx iy\,e^{i\gamma_{E}y}
with the Euler constant
\gamma_{E}\approx 0.577216
, and write
\text{Im}\left[\frac{1}{\Gamma(iy)}\right]\approx y\,\text{cos}(\gamma_{E}y)\approx\text{sin}(y)
for
y\rightarrow 0
or
j\rightarrow j_{0q}
. Therefore, for
j\rightarrow j_{0q}
, we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\approx\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times(-1)\times e\times f_{V}^{+}\times\alpha_{s}(\mu)\times{1\over{Q}}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})-\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)\times\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0q}}\frac{dj}{2}\frac{1-e^{-i\pi j}}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0q}-j)}\right)\,.

Real and imaginary parts

We separate the amplitude (
G
) into its real and imaginary parts

{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\rho^{+}n}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})\equiv{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}=\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]+i\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]\,,

with

\displaystyle\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0q}}\frac{dj}{2}\frac{1-\text{cos}\pi j}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0q}-j)}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

(G.246)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\int_{-\infty}^{j_{0q}}\frac{dj}{2}\,\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}\times\text{sin}\left(\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0q}-j)}\right)\,.

With the change of integration variable
y^{2}=\sqrt{\lambda}(j_{0q}-j)
, we turn the imaginary part into a Gaussian integral that can be evaluated exactly

\displaystyle\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

(G.247)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0q}}}\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dy}{2\pi i\sqrt{\lambda}}\,y\,e^{-\tilde{\tau}y^{2}/\sqrt{\lambda}}\,e^{iy}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\frac{1}{\xi^{j_{0q}}}\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/4\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\,,

with
\tilde{\tau}=\text{log}(1/\xi)
.

We compute the real part of the amplitude
\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]
by first expanding its prefactor near
j=1

\displaystyle\frac{1-\text{cos}\pi j}{{\rm sin}\pi j}\simeq-\frac{2}{\pi(j-1)}+\mathcal{O}(j-1)\,,

(G.248)

and establishing the identity
\partial_{\tilde{\tau}}\left[e^{-\tilde{\tau}}\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]\right]=-(2/\pi)e^{-\tilde{\tau}}\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]
which gives us an approximation for the real part

\displaystyle\text{Re}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{q(valence)}(j_{0q})\times\left(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\,-\,\widehat{\mathbb{F}}^{d}_{j_{0q}(valence)}(\xi,t;\mu^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-1)\times e\,f_{V}^{+}\,\alpha_{s}(\mu)\,{1\over{Q}}\,\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\,\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times(\sqrt{\lambda}/4\pi)^{1/2}\times\frac{1}{\xi}\times\int_{\tilde{\tau}}^{\infty}d\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}\,\frac{2e^{-\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda}-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}}}{\pi\tilde{\tau}^{\prime 3/2}}\,.

Small corrections in the order of
\mathcal{O}(\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]/\text{log}(1/\xi))
and
\mathcal{O}(\text{Im}\left[{\cal A}^{LL(valence)}\right]/\sqrt{\lambda})
to (
G
) can be computed in a standard perturbation series but can be ignored for
\xi\rightarrow 0
and fixed large
\sqrt{\lambda}
.

Small-
\xi
regime

We then compute the integral in (
G
) in the small-
\xi
regime, i.e.,
\tilde{\tau}=\text{log}(1/\xi)\rightarrow\infty
at fixed large
\sqrt{\lambda}
where the integral is dominated by its end point, and can be approximated by

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{\tau}}^{\infty}d\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}\,\frac{2e^{-\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda}-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}^{\prime}}}{\pi\tilde{\tau}^{\prime 3/2}}=(\sqrt{\lambda}/2\pi)\times e^{-4\tilde{\tau}/\sqrt{\lambda}}\times\frac{e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}/4\tilde{\tau}}}{\tilde{\tau}^{3/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\lambda}/\tilde{\tau})\right)\,.

(G.250)

Appendix H
Details of the holographic calculations

A simple way to capture AdS/CFT duality in the non-conformal limit is to model it using a slice of AdS
5
with various bulk fields with assigned
anomalous dimensions and pertinent boundary values, in the so-called bottom-up approach which we will follow here using the conventions in our
recent work in DIS scattering
Mamo and Zahed 2020
;
Mamo and Zahed 2021c
.
We consider AdS
5
with a soft wall with a background metric
g_{MN}=(\eta_{\mu\nu},-1)R^{2}/z^{2}
with the flat metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}=(1,-1,-1,-1)

at the boundary. Confinement will be described by a harmonic background dilaton
\phi={\tilde{\kappa}_{N}}^{2}z^{2}
.

H.1
Bulk vector mesons

The vector mesons fields
L,R
are described by the bulk effective action
Hirn and Sanz 2005
;
Domokos et al. 2009

\displaystyle S_{M}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4g_{5}^{2}}\int d^{5}xe^{-\phi(z)}\sqrt{g}\,g^{MP}g^{NQ}{\rm Tr}\bigg({\cal F}^{L}_{MN}{\cal F}^{L}_{PQ}+{\cal F}^{R}_{MN}{\cal F}^{R}_{PQ}\bigg)+\int d^{5}x\,\bigg(\omega^{L}_{5}({\cal A})-\omega^{R}_{5}({\cal A})\bigg)

(H.251)

with the Chern-Simons contribution

\displaystyle\omega_{5}({\cal A})=\frac{N_{c}}{24\pi^{2}}\int d^{5}x\,{\rm Tr}\left({\cal AF}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\cal A}^{3}{\cal F}-\frac{1}{10}{\cal A}^{5}\right)

(H.252)

Here
{\cal F}=d{\cal A}-i{\cal A}^{2}
and
{\cal A}={\cal A}^{a}T^{a}
with
T^{0}=\frac{3}{4}{\bf 1_{2}}
and
T^{i}=\frac{1}{4}\tau^{i}
, with the form notation subsumed.
Also the vector fields are given by
V=(R+L)/2
and the axial-vector fields are given by
A=(R-L)/2
.
The coupling
g_{5}
in (
H.251
) is fixed by the brane embeddings in bulk, or phenomenologically as

{1}/{g_{5}^{2}}\equiv{N_{c}}/(12\pi^{2})

Cherman et al. 2009
.

The flavor gauge fields solve

\displaystyle\Box V^{\mu}+ze^{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}z^{2}}\partial_{z}\Big(e^{-\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}z^{2}}\frac{1}{z}\partial_{z}V^{\mu}\Big)\,=\,0\qquad\Box V_{z}-\partial_{z}\Big(\partial_{\mu}V^{\mu}\Big)\,=\,0\,.

(H.253)

subject to the gauge condition

\partial_{\mu}V^{\mu}\,+\,ze^{\kappa^{2}z^{2}}\partial_{z}\Big(e^{-\kappa^{2}z^{2}}\frac{1}{z}V_{z}\Big)\,=\,0\,,

(H.254)

with the boundary condition
V_{\mu}(z,y)|_{z\to 0}\,=\,\epsilon_{\mu}(q)\,e^{-iq\cdot y}
.
The non-normalizable solutions are

\displaystyle V_{\mu}(z,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mu}(q)\,e^{-iq\cdot y}\,\Gamma\bigg(1+\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}\bigg)\,\,\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}z^{2}\,\,{\cal U}\bigg(1+\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}};2;\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}z^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle V_{z}(z,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,\epsilon(q)\cdot q\,e^{-iq\cdot y}\,\,\Gamma\bigg(1+\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}\bigg)\,\,z\,\,{\cal U}\bigg(1+\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}};1;\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}\bigg)\,,

(H.255)

with
\,{\cal U}(a;b;w)\,
the confluent hypergeometric functions of the second kind.

H.2
Bulk Dirac fermions

The bulk Dirac fermion action in a sliced of AdS
5
is

\displaystyle S_{F}=\frac{1}{2g_{5}^{2}}\int d^{5}x\,e^{-\phi(z)}\,\sqrt{g}\,\Big(\mathcal{L}_{F1}+\mathcal{L}_{F2}\Big)+\frac{1}{2g_{5}^{2}}\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g^{(4)}}\,\Big(\mathcal{L}_{UV1}+\mathcal{L}_{UV2}\Big)\,,

(H.256)

The Dirac and Pauli contributions to
{\cal L}_{F1,2}
are respectively

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rm Dirac1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{i}{2}\overline{\Psi}_{1,2}e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}\big(\overrightarrow{D}_{N}^{L,R}-\overleftarrow{D}_{N}^{L,R}\big)\Psi_{1,2}-(\pm M+V(z))\bar{\Psi}_{1,2}\Psi_{1,2}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rm Pauli1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm 2g_{5}^{2}\times\eta\,\bar{\Psi}_{1,2}e^{M}_{A}e^{N}_{B}\sigma^{AB}{\cal F}^{L,R}_{MN}\Psi_{1,2}\,,

with
V(z)={\tilde{\kappa}}^{2}z^{2}
,
e^{N}_{A}=z\delta^{N}_{A}
,
\sigma^{AB}=\frac{i}{2}[\Gamma^{A},\Gamma^{B}]
,
and
\omega_{\mu z\nu}=-\omega_{\mu\nu z}=\frac{1}{z}\eta_{\mu\nu}
. The Dirac gamma matrices

\Gamma^{A}=(\gamma^{\mu},-i\gamma^{5})
are chosen in the chiral representation. They satisfy the flat
anti-commutation relation
\{\Gamma^{A},\Gamma^{B}\}=2\eta^{AB}
.
The left and right covariant derivatives are defined as

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{D}_{N}^{X=L,R}=

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\partial}_{N}+\frac{1}{8}\omega_{NAB}[\Gamma^{A},\Gamma^{B}]-iX_{N}^{a}T^{a}\equiv\overrightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}-iX_{N}^{a}T^{a}

\displaystyle\overleftarrow{D}_{N}^{X=L,R}=

\displaystyle\overleftarrow{\partial}_{N}+\frac{1}{8}\omega_{NAB}[\Gamma^{A},\Gamma^{B}]+iX_{N}^{a}T^{a}\equiv\overleftarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}+iX_{N}^{a}T^{a}

(H.258)

The nucleon doublet refers to

\displaystyle\Psi_{1,2}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{p1,2}\\
\Psi_{n1,2}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(H.259)

The nucleon fields in bulk form an iso-doublet
p,n
with
1,2
referring to their boundary chirality
1,2=\pm=R,L

Hong et al. 2007
. They are dual to the boundary sources

\Psi_{p1,2}\leftrightarrow{\cal O}_{p,\pm}
and
\Psi_{n1,2}\leftrightarrow{\cal O}_{n,\pm}
with anomalous dimensions

\pm M=\pm(\Delta-2)=\pm(\tau-3/2)
.

The equation of motions for the bulk Dirac chiral doublet is

\displaystyle\bigg(ie^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}D_{N}^{L,R}-\frac{i}{2}(\partial_{N}\phi)\,e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}-(\pm M+V(z))\bigg)\Psi_{1,2}=0\,,

(H.260)

The normalizable solution to (
H.260
) are

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}(p,z;n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{R}(z;n)\Psi^{0}_{R}(p)+\psi_{L}(z;n)\Psi^{0}_{L}(p)

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}(p,z;n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{R}(z;n)\Psi^{0}_{L}(p)+\psi_{L}(z;n)\Psi^{0}_{R}(p)

(H.261)

with the normalized bulk wave functions

\displaystyle\psi_{R}(z;n)=z^{\Delta}\times\tilde{\psi}_{R}(z;n)=n_{R}{\tilde{\xi}_{N}^{\tau-\frac{3}{2}}}L^{(\tau-2)}_{n}(\tilde{\xi}_{N})\times z^{\Delta}\,,

\displaystyle\psi_{L}(z;n)=z^{\Delta}\times\tilde{\psi}_{L}(z;n)=\tilde{n}_{L}{\tilde{\xi}_{N}^{\tau-1}}L^{(\tau-1)}_{n}(\tilde{\xi}_{N})\times z^{\Delta}\,,

Here
\Delta=\tau+\frac{1}{2}
,
\tilde{\xi}_{N}=\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}z^{2}
,
L_{n}^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{\xi})
are the generalized Laguerre, and
n_{R}=n_{L}\sqrt{\tau-1}

and
n_{L}=\tilde{\kappa}^{-(\tau-2)}\sqrt{{2}/{\Gamma(\tau)}}
. The free Weyl spinors
\Psi^{0}_{R/L}(p)=P_{\pm}u(p)
and
\bar{\Psi}^{0}_{R/L}(p)=\bar{u}(p)P_{\mp}
, and the free boundary spinors satisfy

\displaystyle\bar{u}(p)u(p)=2m_{N}\qquad\qquad 2m_{N}\times\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\mu}u(p)=\bar{u}(p^{\prime})(p^{\prime}+p)^{\mu}u(p)\,.

(H.263)

The fermionic spectrum Reggeizes
m_{n}^{2}=4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}(n+\tau-1)
.
The assignments
1=+
and
2=-
at the boundary are commensurate with the substitutions

\psi_{R,L}\leftrightarrow\mp\psi_{L,R}
by parity.

Using the Dirac 1-form currents

\displaystyle J^{aN}_{L}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{\rm Dirac1}}{\partial L^{a}_{N}}=\overline{\Psi}_{1}e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}T^{a}\Psi_{1}\,,

\displaystyle J^{aN}_{R}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{\rm Dirac2}}{\partial R^{a}_{N}}=\overline{\Psi}_{2}e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}T^{a}\Psi_{2}\,,

(H.264)

and Pauli 2-form currents

\displaystyle J^{aMN}_{L}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{\rm Pauli1}}{\partial L^{a}_{MN}}=+2g_{5}^{2}\times\eta^{a}\overline{\Psi}_{1}e^{M}_{A}e^{N}_{B}\sigma^{AB}T^{a}\Psi_{1}\,,

\displaystyle J^{aMN}_{R}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{\rm Pauli2}}{\partial R^{a}_{MN}}=-2g_{5}^{2}\times\eta^{a}\overline{\Psi}_{2}e^{M}_{A}e^{N}_{B}\sigma^{AB}T^{a}\Psi_{2}\,.

(H.265)

we can rewrite (
H.2
) with the explicit isoscalar (
a=0
) and isovector (
a=3
) contributions

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F1}+\mathcal{L}_{F2}\supset

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{1}e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}\big(\overrightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}-\overleftarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}\big)\Psi_{1}-(M+V(z))\bar{\Psi}_{1}\Psi_{1}+\frac{i}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{2}e^{N}_{A}\Gamma^{A}\big(\overrightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}-\overleftarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{N}\big)\Psi_{2}-(-M+V(z))\bar{\Psi}_{2}\Psi_{2}

\displaystyle+V_{N}^{0}J_{V}^{0N}+A_{N}^{0}J_{A}^{0N}+V_{N}^{3}J_{V}^{3N}+A_{N}^{3}J_{A}^{3N}+V^{0}_{MN}J_{V}^{0MN}+A^{0}_{MN}J_{A}^{0MN}+V^{3}_{MN}J_{V}^{3MN}+A^{3}_{MN}J_{A}^{3MN}

with
J_{V,A}^{aN}=J_{L}^{aN}\pm J_{R}^{aN}
and
J_{V,A}^{aMN}=J_{L}^{aMN}\mp J_{R}^{aMN}
.

H.3
Bulk glueballs

The graviton in the bulk of AdS space is dual to a glueball on the boundary. The graviton tensor
h_{\mu\nu}
can be decomposed into its transverse and traceless part,
h
, and its trace-full part,
f
, using
Kanitscheider et al. 2008
. The decomposition is given by

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}=\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{TT}\,h+\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{T}\,f-k_{\mu}k_{\nu}H+k_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{\perp}+k_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{\perp}\,

where
k^{\mu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{TT}=\eta^{\mu\nu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{TT}=0
, and
\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{T}=\frac{1}{4}\eta_{\mu\nu}
. In a gauge where
A_{\mu}^{\perp}=0
, the equation of motion for
h
decouples, but the equations for
f
,
H
, and
\varphi
are coupled (see Eqs.7.16-20 in
Kanitscheider et al. 2008
). Diagonalizing the equations shows that
f
satisfies the same equation of motion as
h

Kanitscheider et al. 2008
. It’s important to note that
f
couples to
T^{\mu}_{\mu}
of the gauge theory, while
H
couples to
k^{\mu}k^{\nu}T_{\mu\nu}\equiv 0
(see Eq.7.6 of
Kanitscheider et al. 2008
).

The effective action for the graviton (
\eta_{\mu\nu}\rightarrow\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}
) and dilaton fluctuations (
\phi\rightarrow\phi+\varphi
) follows from the Einstein-Hilbert action plus dilaton by expanding to quadratic order, and after adding the background de-Donder gauge fixing term. The result is

\displaystyle S=\int d^{5}x\sqrt{g}\,e^{-2\phi}\big(\mathcal{L}_{h+f}+\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}\big)\,,

(H.268)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{h+f}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\tilde{g}_{5}^{2}}\,g^{\mu\nu}\,\eta^{\lambda\rho}\eta^{\sigma\tau}\partial_{\mu}h_{\lambda\sigma}\partial_{\nu}h_{\rho\tau}+\frac{1}{8\tilde{g}_{5}^{2}}\,g^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\alpha\beta}\eta^{\gamma\sigma}\,\partial_{\mu}h_{\alpha\beta}\,\partial_{\nu}h_{\gamma\sigma}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\tilde{g}_{5}^{2}}\,g^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\varphi\,\partial_{\nu}\varphi\,,

(H.269)

and
\tilde{g}_{5}^{2}=2\kappa^{2}=16\pi G_{N}={8\pi^{2}}/{N_{c}^{2}}
. Here
g_{\mu\nu}
is the AdS metric.

For the graviton in the axial gauge
h_{\mu z}=h_{zz}=0
.
The pertinent couplings follow from linearizing the action (
H.256
) by replacing
\eta_{\mu\nu}\rightarrow\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}
, are

\displaystyle h\overline{\Psi}\Psi:\quad

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}}{2}\int d^{5}x\,\sqrt{g}\,h_{\mu\nu}T_{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle hAA:\quad

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}}{2}\int d^{5}x\,\sqrt{g}\,h_{\mu\nu}T_{V}^{\mu\nu}

with the energy-momentum tensors

\displaystyle T_{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\phi}\frac{i}{2}\,z\,\overline{\Psi}\gamma^{\mu}\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial^{\nu}}\Psi-\eta^{\mu\nu}\mathcal{L}_{F}\,,

\displaystyle T_{V}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{-\phi}\Big(z^{4}\eta^{\rho\sigma}\eta^{\mu\beta}\eta^{\nu\gamma}\,F^{V}_{\beta\rho}F^{V}_{\gamma\sigma}-z^{4}\,\eta^{\mu\beta}\eta^{\nu\gamma}\,F^{V}_{\beta z}F^{V}_{\gamma z}\Big)-\eta^{\mu\nu}\mathcal{L}_{V}\,.

(H.271)

H.4
t-channel spin-2 glueball exchange

In the soft-wall model the normalized wave function for spin-2 glueballs is given by
Ballon Bayona et al. 2008
(note that the discussion in
Ballon Bayona et al. 2008
is for general massive bulk scalar fluctuation but can be used for spin-2 glueball which has an effective bulk action similar to massless bulk scalar fluctuation)

\displaystyle J_{h}(m_{n},z)\equiv\psi_{n}(z)=c_{n}\,z^{4}L_{n}^{2}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})\,,

with
\tilde{\xi}_{T}=\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}z^{2}
, and

\displaystyle c_{n}=\Bigg(\frac{2^{4}\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{6}\Gamma(n+1)}{\Gamma(n+3)}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{4\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{3}}{\sqrt{(n+2)(n+1)}}\,,

(H.273)

which is determined from the normalization condition (for soft-wall model with background dilaton
\phi=\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}z^{2}
)

\displaystyle\int dz\,\sqrt{g}e^{-\phi}\,{\left|g^{xx}\right|}\,\psi_{n}(z)\psi_{m}(z)=\delta_{nm}\,.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle F_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\kappa}\bigg(-\frac{1}{z^{\prime 3}}\partial_{z^{\prime}}\psi_{n}(z^{\prime})\bigg)_{z^{\prime}=\epsilon}=-\frac{4}{\sqrt{2}\kappa}c_{n}L_{n}^{2}(0)\,,

with
\psi_{n}(z\rightarrow 0)\approx c_{n}\,z^{4}L_{n}^{2}(0)
and with
L_{n}^{2}(0)=\binom{2+n}{n}
. We can also re-write the normalized wave function of glueballs (
H.324
) in terms of
F_{n}
as

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(z)=-\sqrt{2}\kappa\times F_{n}\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{4}}\times\frac{1}{L_{n}^{2}(0)}\times\tilde{\xi}_{T}^{2}L_{n}^{2}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})\,,

(H.276)

Also note that
m_{n}^{2}=8\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}(n+2)
,

\displaystyle F_{n}^{2}=\frac{16}{2\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{16\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{6}}{(n+2)(n+1)}\times\left(L_{n}^{2}(0)\right)^{2}\,.

(H.277)

For space-like momenta (
t=k^{2}=-K^{2}
), we have the bulk-to-bulk propagator near the boundary

\displaystyle G(z\rightarrow 0,z^{\prime})\approx\frac{z^{4}}{4}\sum_{n}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}=\frac{z^{4}}{4}\mathcal{H}(K,z^{\prime}),

(H.278)

where, for the soft-wall model
Abidin and Carlson 2008
;
Ballon Bayona et al. 2008

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(K,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,,

(H.279)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4z^{4}\Gamma(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+2)U\Big(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+2,3;2\tilde{\xi}_{T}\Big)=\Gamma(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+2)U\Big(\frac{a_{K}}{2},-1;2\tilde{\xi}_{T}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+2)}{\Gamma(\frac{a_{K}}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,x^{\frac{a_{K}}{2}-1}(1-x){\rm exp}\Big(-\frac{x}{1-x}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})\Big)\,,

with
\tilde{\xi}_{T}=\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}z^{2}
,
a_{K}={K^{2}}/{4\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}}
, and we have used the transformation
U(m,n;y)=y^{1-n}U(1+m-n,2-n,y)
. (
H.279
) satisfies the normalization condition
{\cal H}(0,z)={\cal H}(K,0)=1
.

For example, the t-channel spin-2 glueball exchange contribution to electroproduction of vector mesons, is given by

\displaystyle i{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma^{*}_{T/L}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t)=\sum_{n}i\tilde{{\cal A}}^{h}_{\gamma^{*}_{T/L}p\rightarrow Vp}(m_{n},s,t)

\displaystyle i\tilde{{\cal A}}^{h}_{\gamma_{T/L}^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(m_{n},s,t)=\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times(-i)V_{h\gamma^{*}_{T/L}V}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},k,m_{n})\times\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\times(-i)V_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}^{\alpha\beta}(p_{1},p_{2},k,m_{n})\,,

with the bulk vertices (defining
t=k^{2}=\Delta^{2}
, and
k=\Delta=p_{2}-p_{1}=q-q^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle V_{h\gamma^{*}_{T/L}V}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},k,m_{n})\equiv\left(\frac{\delta S_{h\gamma^{*}_{T/L}V}^{k}}{\delta(\epsilon_{\mu\nu}h(k,z))}\right)\,J_{h}(m_{n},z)=\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\int dz\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z^{4}K_{T/L}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},z)J_{h}(m_{n},z)\,,

\displaystyle V_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}^{\alpha\beta}(p_{1},p_{2},k,m_{n})\equiv\left(\frac{\delta S_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}^{k}}{\delta(\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}h(k,z))}\right)\,J_{h}(m_{n},z)=-\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\int dz\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\bar{\Psi}(p_{2},z)\gamma^{\alpha}p^{\beta}\Psi(p_{1},z)J_{h}(m_{n},z)\,,

(H.281)

where we have defined the kinematic factors (in the high energy limit
s\gg-t
with
p=({p_{1}+p_{2}})/{2}\approx p_{1}
, and
q^{\mu}\approx q^{\prime\mu}
) as

\displaystyle K_{T}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},\epsilon_{T},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T},z)\approx-q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\times\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)\equiv q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\times K_{T}(Q,M_{V},z)\,,

\displaystyle K_{L}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},\epsilon_{L},\epsilon^{\prime}_{L},z)\approx-q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\times\frac{1}{Q\,M_{V}}\times\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)\equiv q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\times K_{L}(Q,M_{V},z)\,,

(H.282)

using

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\xi}\,\Gamma(1+a_{Q})\,\,{\cal U}(1+a_{Q};2;\tilde{\xi})=\tilde{\xi}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dx}{(1-x)^{2}}x^{a_{Q}}{\rm exp}\Big(-\frac{x\,\tilde{\xi}}{1-x}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(z)=g_{5}\times\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times 2\tilde{\xi}L_{0}^{1}(\tilde{\xi})\,,

(H.283)

with
a_{Q}=Q^{2}/(4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2})
and
\tilde{\xi}=\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}
.

The full bulk-to-bulk spin-2 glueball (graviton) propagator
G_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k,z,z^{\prime})
is given by
Raju 2011
;
D’Hoker et al. 1999

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k,z,z^{\prime})=J_{h}(m_{n},z)\times\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\times J_{h}(m_{n},z^{\prime})\,,

where the massive spin-2 boundary propagator
\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)=P_{\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\times\frac{-i}{k^{2}-m_{n}^{2}+i\epsilon}\,,

(H.285)

with the massive spin-2 projection operator
P_{\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)
defined as

\displaystyle P_{\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)={1\over 2}\left(P_{\mu;\alpha}P_{\nu;\beta}+P_{\mu;\beta}P_{\nu;\alpha}-\frac{2}{3}P_{\mu;\nu}P_{\alpha;\beta}\right)\,,

(H.286)

which is written in terms of the massive spin-1 projection operator

\displaystyle P_{\mu;\alpha}(m_{n},k)=\eta_{\mu\alpha}-k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}/m_{n}^{2}\,.

(H.287)

For
z\rightarrow 0
,
t=-K^{2}
, and focusing on the transverse part of the boundary propagator, i.e.,

\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\approx\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}\times\frac{-i}{k^{2}-m_{n}^{2}+i\epsilon}

(as we did in
Mamo and Zahed 2020
), we can simplify (
H.4
) as

\displaystyle i{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma^{*}_{T/L}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t)\approx\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times(-i)\mathcal{V}^{\mu\nu}_{h\gamma^{*}_{T/L}V}(q,q^{\prime},k)\times\bigg(\frac{-i}{2}\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}\bigg)\times(-i)\mathcal{V}^{\alpha\beta}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(p_{1},p_{2},k)\,,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\mu\nu}_{h\gamma^{*}_{T/L}V}(q,q^{\prime},k_{)}=\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\int dz\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z^{4}K_{T/L}^{\mu\nu}(q,q^{\prime},\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime},z)\frac{z^{4}}{4}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\alpha\beta}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{z})=-\sqrt{2\kappa^{2}}\times\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\bar{\Psi}(p_{2},z)\gamma^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,\Psi(p_{1},z)\mathcal{H}(K,z)\,.

(H.289)

However, if we instead use the full massive spin-2 boundary propagator
\tilde{G}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)
(
H.285
), we find (particularly for longitudinal vector meson production)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma_{L}^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\frac{1}{QM_{V}}\times\mathcal{V}_{h\gamma_{L}^{*}V}(Q,M_{V})\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\left[q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\,P_{\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\,p_{1}^{\alpha}p_{1}^{\beta}\right]\times\mathcal{V}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(K,m_{n})\,,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{h\gamma^{*}_{L}V}(Q,M_{V})=-\frac{1}{2}\int dz\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z^{4}\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)\frac{z^{4}}{4}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(K,m_{n})=-\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,.

Following
Nishio and
Watari 2014b
, we can evaluate

\displaystyle q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\,P_{\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}(m_{n},k)\,p_{1}^{\alpha}p_{1}^{\beta}=\left(p_{1}\cdot q\right)^{2}\times\left(1+\frac{4}{3}\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{m_{n}^{2}}\times\eta^{2}\right)\,,

(H.292)

using the general result (see Eq.279 in
Nishio and
Watari 2014b
)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}q^{\mu_{2}}...q^{\mu_{j}}\,P_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}...\mu_{j};\nu_{1}\nu_{2}...\nu_{j}}(m_{n},k)\,p_{1}^{\nu_{1}}p_{1}^{\nu_{2}}...p_{1}^{\nu_{j}}=\left(p_{1}\cdot q\right)^{j}\times\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})

(H.293)

where
\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})
, for even
j=2,4,...
, is a polynomial of skewness
\eta
of degree
j
which can be written explicitly in terms of the hypergeometric function
{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,d)
as

\displaystyle\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})=\,_{2}F_{1}\left(-\frac{j}{2},\frac{1-j}{2};\frac{1}{2}-j;-\frac{4p_{1}^{2}}{m_{n}^{2}}\times\eta^{2}\right)\,.

(H.294)

Note that we have also replaced
p^{2}\sim p_{1}^{2}
by
-p_{1}^{2}
, and
-\Delta^{2}
by
m_{n}^{2}
in Eq.279 of
Nishio and
Watari 2014b
, since we are using the massive spin-1 projection operator
P_{\mu;\alpha}(m_{n},k)=-\eta_{\mu\alpha}+k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}/m_{n}^{2}
instead of the massless one
P_{\mu;\alpha}(\Delta=k)=\eta_{\mu\alpha}-k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}/\Delta^{2}
used in
Mamo and Zahed 2020
for the conformal case with
\eta_{\mu\alpha}=(-,+,+,+)
signature.

Therefore, using (
H.292
) in (
H.4
), we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma_{L}^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{g_{5}^{2}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\frac{1}{QM_{V}}\times\mathcal{V}_{h\gamma_{L}^{*}V}(Q,M_{V})\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(p_{1}\cdot q\right)^{2}\times\left[A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})+\eta^{2}D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

with the A and D gravitational form factors
Mamo and Zahed 2020
;
Mamo and Zahed 2021a
;
Mamo and Zahed 2022

\displaystyle A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,,

(H.296)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\mathcal{H}(K,z)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(0)\times 6\times\frac{\Gamma\left(2+\frac{a_{K}}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(4+\frac{a_{K}}{2}\right)}\times\,_{2}F_{1}\left(3,\frac{a_{K}}{2};\frac{a_{K}}{2}+4;-1\right)\,,

and

\displaystyle D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{4}{3}\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{m_{n}^{2}}\times\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,,

(H.297)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{4}{3}\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{k^{2}}\times\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z)}{-k^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{3}\frac{m_{N}^{2}}{K^{2}}\times A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})\,.

Following our arguments in
Mamo and Zahed 2021d
;
Mamo and Zahed 2022
, we also replace

\displaystyle D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})\rightarrow D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{3}\frac{m_{N}^{2}}{K^{2}}\times\left[A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})-A_{S}(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

(H.298)

where

\displaystyle A_{S}(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(0)\times 6\times\frac{\Gamma\left(2+\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(4+\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}\right)}\times\,_{2}F_{1}\left(3,\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2};\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}+4;-1\right)\,,

(H.299)

with
\tilde{a}_{K}=K^{2}/4\tilde{\kappa}_{S}^{2}
.

H.5
t-channel spin-j glueball exchange

In the soft-wall model, the spin-j glueballs’ normalized wave functions are given in terms of the generalized Laguerre polynomials as
Mamo and Zahed 2020
;
Mamo and Zahed 2021a

\displaystyle\psi_{n}(j,z)=c_{n}(j)\,z^{\Delta}L_{n}^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})\,,

(H.300)

where
\tilde{\xi}_{T}=\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}z^{2}
, and the normalization coefficients are

\displaystyle c_{n}(j)=\Big(\frac{2^{\Delta_{g}(j)}\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2(\Delta_{g}(j)-1)}\Gamma(n+1)}{\Gamma(n+\Delta_{g}(j)-1)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

(H.301)

with

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}(j)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2+\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0g})}\,,

(H.302)

where
j_{0g}=2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}
for closed strings.

The non-normalized bulk-to-boundary propagators for spin-j glueballs are given in terms of Kummer’s (confluent hypergeometric) function of the second kind and its integral representation as (for space-like momenta
k^{2}=-K^{2}
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(j,K,z)

\displaystyle=z^{\Delta}U\Big(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2},\Delta_{g}(j)-1;2\tilde{\xi}_{T}\Big)=z^{\Delta_{g}(j)}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})^{2-\Delta_{g}(j)}U\Big(\tilde{a}(j),\tilde{b}(j);2\tilde{\xi}_{T}\Big)

(H.303)

\displaystyle=z^{\Delta_{g}(j)}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})^{2-\Delta_{g}(j)}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\tilde{a}(j))}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,x^{\tilde{a}(j)-1}(1-x)^{-\tilde{b}(j)}{\rm exp}\Big(-\frac{x}{1-x}(2\tilde{\xi}_{T})\Big)\,,

where

\displaystyle a_{K}=\frac{K^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}}\qquad\tilde{a}(j)=\frac{a_{K}}{2}+2-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\qquad\tilde{b}(j)=3-\Delta_{g}(j)

(H.304)

and we have used the transformation
U(m,n;y)=y^{1-n}U(1+m-n,2-n,y)
.
The bulk-to-bulk propagator can also be approximated as (for space-like momenta
k^{2}=-K^{2}
)

\displaystyle G(j,z\rightarrow 0,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\psi_{n}(j,z\rightarrow 0)}{(\sqrt{2}\kappa)\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)}\times(\sqrt{2}\kappa)\sum_{n}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\Gamma(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2})}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2\Delta_{g}(j)-4}\times\frac{z^{\Delta_{g}(j)}}{\Delta_{g}(j)}\times\mathcal{H}(j,K,z^{\prime}),

where we used

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(j,K,z^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{C}(j,K,\epsilon)}{\sqrt{2}\kappa}\times(-\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,{\left|g^{xx}\right|}\,\partial_{z^{\prime}}\psi_{n}(j,z^{\prime}))|_{z^{\prime}=\epsilon}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(j,K,\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(j,K,\epsilon)\,,

(H.306)

with
\psi_{n}(j,z\rightarrow 0)\approx c_{n}(j)\,z^{\Delta}L_{n}^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}(0)
for the soft-wall model.

For example, the electroproduction of vector mesons (using the spin-j glueball bulk-to-boundary propagator (
)) is given by
Mamo and Zahed 2021a

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma^{*}_{L/T}p\rightarrow Vp}(j,s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\mathcal{V}^{\mu\nu}_{h\gamma^{*}_{L/T}V}(j,Q,M_{V})\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\left[q^{\mu_{1}}q^{\mu_{2}}...q^{\mu_{j}}\,P_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}...\mu_{j};\nu_{1}\nu_{2}...\nu_{j}}(m_{n},k)\,p_{1}^{\nu_{1}}p_{1}^{\nu_{2}}...p_{1}^{\nu_{j}}\right]\times\mathcal{V}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(j,K,m_{n})\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{h\gamma^{*}_{L/T}V}(j,Q,M_{V})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{4+2(j-2)}

\displaystyle\times K_{T/L}(Q,M_{V})\times\frac{z^{\Delta_{g}(j)-(j-2)}}{\Delta_{g}(j)}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2\Delta_{g}(j)-4}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{h\bar{\Psi}\Psi}(j,K,m_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{1+2(j-2)}\,\big(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\big)\,z^{-(j-2)}

(H.308)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}\times 2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{j-2+\Delta_{g}(j)}\,,

with

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}(j)=2+\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0g})}\,,\qquad\frac{a_{K}}{2}=\frac{K^{2}}{8{\tilde{\kappa}}_{T}^{2}}\,,\qquad j_{0g}=2-\frac{2}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,,

(H.309)

and

\displaystyle K_{T}(Q,M_{V},z)\equiv-\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)\,,

\displaystyle K_{L}(Q,M_{V},z)\equiv-\frac{1}{Q\,M_{V}}\times\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(Q,z)\partial_{z}\mathcal{V}_{V}(M_{V},z)\,.

(H.310)

Finally using (
H.293
)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}q^{\mu_{2}}...q^{\mu_{j}}\,P_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}...\mu_{j};\nu_{1}\nu_{2}...\nu_{j}}(m_{n},k)\,p_{1}^{\nu_{1}}p_{1}^{\nu_{2}}...p_{1}^{\nu_{j}}=\left(p_{1}\cdot q\right)^{j}\times\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})

(H.311)

in (
H.5
), we find

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{h}_{\gamma^{*}_{L/T}p\rightarrow Vp}(j,s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\mathcal{V}^{\mu\nu}_{h\gamma^{*}_{L/T}V}(j,Q,M_{V})\times\left(p_{1}\cdot q\right)^{j}\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})+\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

where we have defined the spin-j moments (or spin-j form factors), for even
j=2,4,..
, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{1+2(j-2)}\,\big(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\big)\,z^{-(j-2)}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}\times 2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{j-2+\Delta_{g}(j)}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{1+2(j-2)}\,\big(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\big)\,z^{-(j-2)}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{H}(j,K,z)\times 2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\times\Gamma\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{j-2+\Delta_{g}(j)}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{1-\Delta_{g}(j)}}{\Gamma(\tau)}\Bigg((\tau-1)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau-1\right)

\displaystyle\times

\,{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+1;-1\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau\right)

\displaystyle\times

{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+2;-1\right)\Bigg)\,,

where
{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}
is the regularized hypergeometric function,
and we have defined the generalized
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}
-terms at finite skewness
\eta\neq 0
and even
j=2,4,...
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{1+2(j-2)}\,\big(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\big)\,z^{-(j-2)}

(H.314)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})-1\right)\times\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}

\displaystyle\times 2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\times\Gamma\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{j-2+\Delta_{g}(j)}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,z^{1+2(j-2)}\,\big(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\big)\,z^{-(j-2)}

\displaystyle\times\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,k^{2})-1\right)\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa\mathcal{F}_{n}(j)\psi_{n}(j,z)}{-k^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}

\displaystyle\times 2^{\Delta_{g}(j)-2}\times\Gamma\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{j-2+\Delta_{g}(j)}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,-K^{2})-1\right)\times\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})\,.

Generalizing our arguments in
Mamo and Zahed 2021a
for
j=2
to an arbitrary even
j\geq 2
, we replace

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

(H.315)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,-K^{2})-1\right)\times\left[\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})-\mathcal{A}_{S}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{S}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\,.

(H.316)

H.6
t-channel spin-1 meson exchange

In the soft-wall model the normalized wave function for spin-1 (vector) mesons is given by
Grigoryan and Radyushkin 2007

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(z)=c_{n}\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}L_{n}^{1}(\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2})\equiv J_{V}(m_{n},z)\,,

with
c_{n}=\sqrt{{2}/{(n+1)}}
which is determined from the normalization condition (for the soft-wall model with background dilaton
\phi=\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}
)

\displaystyle\int dz\,\sqrt{g}e^{-\phi}\,(g^{xx})^{2}\,\phi_{n}(z)\phi_{m}(z)=\delta_{nm}\,.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle F_{n}=\frac{1}{g_{5}}\bigg(-e^{-\phi}\frac{1}{z^{\prime}}\partial_{z^{\prime}}\phi_{n}(z^{\prime})\bigg)_{z^{\prime}=\epsilon}=-\frac{2}{g_{5}}c_{n}(n+1)\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}\,,

with
\phi_{n}(z\rightarrow 0)\approx c_{n}\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}(n+1)
. If we define the decay constant as
f_{n}=-{F_{n}}/{m_{n}}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{n}(z)=g_{5}\times\frac{f_{n}}{m_{n}}\times 2\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}L_{n}^{1}(\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2})\,.

(H.320)

Note that for
z\rightarrow 0
, we can write the bulk-to-bulk propagator as

\displaystyle G(z\rightarrow 0,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\phi_{n}(z\rightarrow 0)}{-g_{5}F_{n}}\sum_{n}\frac{-g_{5}F_{n}\phi_{n}(z^{\prime})}{k^{2}-m_{n}^{2}}=\frac{z^{2}}{2}\sum_{n}\frac{-g_{5}F_{n}\phi_{n}(z^{\prime})}{k^{2}-m_{n}^{2}}=\frac{z^{2}}{2}V(k,z^{\prime}).

For space-like momenta (
t=k^{2}=-K^{2}
), we have the bulk-to-bulk propagator near the boundary

\displaystyle G(z\rightarrow 0,z^{\prime})\approx\frac{z^{2}}{2}\sum_{n}\frac{g_{5}F_{n}\phi_{n}(z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}=\frac{z^{2}}{2}\mathcal{V}(K,z^{\prime})\,,

(H.322)

where
Grigoryan and Radyushkin 2007

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(K,z)=g_{5}\sum_{n}\frac{F_{n}\phi_{n}(z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}=\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dx}{(1-x)^{2}}x^{a_{K}}{\rm exp}\Big[-\frac{x}{1-x}\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}\Big]\,,

(H.323)

with the normalization
{\cal V}(0,z)={\cal V}(K,0)=1
, and we have defined
a_{K}=\frac{K^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}
.

H.7
t-channel spin-j meson exchange

The spin-1 transverse bulk gauge field defined as
zV_{\mu}(m_{n},z)
obeys the same bulk equation of motion as a bulk massive scalar field
\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j=1,z)
with
m^{2}R^{2}=-3
. Therefore, the spin-j normalized meson wave functions
J_{V}(m_{n}(j),z)
can be expressed in terms of the wave functions of massive scalar
fields
\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z)
which are given, for the soft wall model, in terms of the generalized Laguerre polynomials as
Ballon Bayona et al. 2008

\displaystyle zJ_{V}(m_{n}(j),z)\equiv\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z)=c_{n}(j)\,z^{\Delta_{q}(j)}L_{n}^{\Delta_{q}(j)-2}(\tilde{\xi})\,,

(H.324)

with
\tilde{\xi}=\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}
. The normalization coefficients are

\displaystyle c_{n}(j)=\Big(\frac{2\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2(\Delta_{q}(j)-1)}\Gamma(n+1)}{\Gamma(n+\Delta_{q}(j)-1)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

(H.325)

and the dimension of the massive scalar fields (with an additional mass coming from the massive open string states attached to the D9 or D7-branes)
\Delta_{q}(j)
is given by

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}(j)=2+\sqrt{4+m^{2}R^{2}+\frac{R^{2}}{\alpha^{\prime}}(j-1)}=2+\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0q})}\,,

(H.326)

where, in the last line, we have used the fact that
m^{2}R^{2}=-3
, the open string quantized mass spectrum
m_{j}^{2}R^{2}=(j-1)({R^{2}}/{\alpha^{\prime}})=\sqrt{\lambda}(j-1)
for open strings attached to the D9-brane in bulk, and we have defined
j_{0q}=1-{1}/{\sqrt{\lambda}}
.

We now recall that the non-normalized bulk-to-boundary propagators of massive scalar fields are given in terms of Kummer’s (confluent hypergeometric) function of the second kind, and their integral representations are (for space-like momenta
k^{2}=-K^{2}
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{V}}(j,K,z)=

\displaystyle z^{\Delta_{q}(j)}U\Big(a_{K}+\frac{\Delta_{q}(j)}{2},\Delta_{q}(j)-1;\tilde{\xi}\Big)=z^{\Delta_{q}(j)}\tilde{\xi}^{2-\Delta_{q}(j)}U\Big(\hat{a}(j),\hat{b}(j);\tilde{\xi}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z^{\Delta_{q}(j)}\tilde{\xi}^{2-\Delta_{q}(j)}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\tilde{a}(j))}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,x^{\hat{a}(j)-1}(1-x)^{-\hat{b}(j)}{\rm exp}\Big(-\frac{x}{1-x}\tilde{\xi}\Big)\,,

(H.327)

with
\tilde{\xi}=\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}z^{2}

\displaystyle a_{K}=\frac{K^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}\qquad\hat{a}(j)=a_{K}+2-\frac{\Delta_{q}(j)}{2}\qquad\hat{b}(j)=3-\Delta_{q}(j)

(H.328)

after using the identity
U(m,n;y)=y^{1-n}U(1+m-n,2-n,y)
.
Therefore, the bulk-to-bulk propagator of spin-j mesons (defined as massive bulk scalar fields) can be approximated at the boundary as (for space-like momenta
k^{2}=-K^{2}
)

\displaystyle\tilde{G}(j,z\rightarrow 0,z^{\prime})\approx

\displaystyle-\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z\rightarrow 0)}{g_{5}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n}(j)}\bigg]\times\sum_{n}\frac{g_{5}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n}(j)\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2})^{\Delta_{q}(j)-2}\frac{z^{\Delta_{q}(j)-1}}{\Delta_{q}(j)-1}\frac{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2+a_{K})}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}\,\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(j,K,z^{\prime})

where we have defined the non-normalized bulk-to-boundary propagator of spin-j mesons (which are defined as massive spin-j scalar fields)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(j,K,z^{\prime})=\sum_{n}\frac{g_{5}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n}(j)\phi_{n}(j,z^{\prime})}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}(j)}\,,

with the mass spectrum of massive spin-j scalar fields
m_{n}^{2}(j)=4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}(n+\frac{\Delta_{q}(j)}{2})
, and we have also defined

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{n}(j)=

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(j,K,\epsilon)}{g_{5}}\bigg(-\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}\,\big(g^{xx}\big)^{2}\,\partial_{z^{\prime}}\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z^{\prime})\bigg)_{z^{\prime}=\epsilon}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(j,K,\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(j,K,\epsilon)

(H.331)

with
\tilde{\phi}_{n}(j,z\rightarrow 0)\approx c_{n}(j)\,z^{\Delta_{q}(j)-2}L_{n}^{\Delta_{q}(j)-2}(0)
for the soft wall model.

Note that the bulk-to-boundary propagator of spin-j mesons (
H.7
) is equivalent to the spin-j glueball bulk-to-boundary propagator (
H.327
) with the replacements:
j\rightarrow j+1,\Delta_{g}\rightarrow\Delta_{q},\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V}
in (
H.327
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}(j,K,z;\Delta_{q};\tilde{\kappa_{V}})\equiv\mathcal{H}(j\rightarrow j+1,K,z;\Delta_{g}\rightarrow\Delta_{q};\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\,.

H.8
Electroproduction of vector mesons with t-channel spin-j closed string exchange in AdS

Spin-2 glueball t-exchange

The diffractive production vector mesons in holography is mediated by bulk spin-2 gravitons near threshold, and their Reggeized
contribution to the Pomeron away from treshold.
The t-channel spin-2 graviton (spin-2 glueball resonances) contribution to the transverse and longitudinal vector meson production amplitude at finite skewness,
\eta\neq 0
, is illustrated in Fig.
1
(right) with the result

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{TT}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t,Q,M_{V},\epsilon_{T},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\left(\frac{s}{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\mathcal{I}(Q,M_{V})\times\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{4}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{4}\times\left[A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})+\eta^{2}D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(s,t,Q,M_{V},\epsilon_{L},\epsilon^{\prime}_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\left(\frac{s}{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\mathcal{I}(Q,M_{V})\times\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}^{4}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{4}\times\frac{1}{3}\times\frac{Q}{M_{V}}\times\left[A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})+\eta^{2}D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

A detailed account of this result can be found in
Mamo and Zahed 2021a
to which we refer for completeness.
The spin-2 gravitational form factors
A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})
and
D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})
are given by

\displaystyle A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa F_{n}\psi_{n}(z)}{K^{2}+m_{n}^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int dz\,\sqrt{g}\,e^{-\phi}z\,\left(\psi_{R}^{2}(z)+\psi_{L}^{2}(z)\right)\times\mathcal{H}(K,z)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\times\frac{\Gamma\left(2+\frac{a_{K}}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(4+\frac{a_{K}}{2}\right)}\times\,_{2}F_{1}\left(3,\frac{a_{K}}{2};\frac{a_{K}}{2}+4;-1\right)\,,

\displaystyle D(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{3}\frac{m_{N}^{2}}{K^{2}}\times\left[A(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})-A_{S}(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

with

\displaystyle A_{S}(K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(0)\times 6\times\frac{\Gamma\left(2+\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(4+\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}\right)}\times\,_{2}F_{1}\left(3,\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2};\frac{\tilde{a}_{K}}{2}+4;-1\right)\,,

(H.335)

with
\tilde{a}_{K}=K^{2}/4\tilde{\kappa}_{S}^{2}
.

The bulk-to-boundary vector propagators associated to the incoming virtual photon, and outgoing vector meson
in Fig.
1
, set the scale factors in (
H.8
) namely (
\hat{\xi}\equiv Qz
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}{Q}\Big)^{4}\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\hat{\xi}^{-1}\times\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\mathcal{V}_{V}(\hat{\xi}M_{V}/Q)\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{4}}{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\Bigg(\frac{3}{\frac{1}{32}\frac{Q^{6}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{6}}+\frac{3}{4}\frac{Q^{4}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{4}}+\frac{11}{2}\frac{Q^{2}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+12}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\left(\frac{3}{\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+3\right)\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+2\right)\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+1\right)}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}{Q}\Big)^{4}\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\hat{\xi}^{-1}\times\partial_{\hat{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\times\partial_{\hat{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{V}(\hat{\xi}M_{V}/Q)\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{4}}{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\Bigg(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{32}\frac{Q^{6}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{6}}+\frac{3}{4}\frac{Q^{4}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{4}}+\frac{11}{2}\frac{Q^{2}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{6}}+12}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\times\mathcal{I}(Q,M_{V})\,,

(H.336)

Spin-j glueball t-exchange

Away from threshold, the diffractive electroproduction of vector mesons involve heavier spin-j
gravitons which are the holographic dual of spin-j glueballs at the boundary. More specifically,
the spin-j glueball exchange contribution to Fig.
1
(right) can be written as

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{TT}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(j,s,t,Q,M_{V},\epsilon_{T},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T})=\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{g_{5}}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\,\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}\Big)^{8-3\Delta_{g}(j)+j-2}\times\frac{4}{\Delta_{g}(j)}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{4^{j/2}}\times\mathcal{I}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\times\left[\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K)\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow Vp}(j,s,t,Q,M_{V},\epsilon_{L},\epsilon^{\prime}_{L})=\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{g_{5}}\times\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\,\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{T}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}\Big)^{8-3\Delta_{g}(j)+j-2}\times\frac{4}{\Delta_{g}(j)}\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{g}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{4^{j/2}}\times\frac{2}{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}\times\frac{Q}{M_{V}}\times\mathcal{I}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\times\left[\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K)\right]\times\frac{1}{m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

for even
j=2,4...
, with the spin-j form factors

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{2^{1-\Delta_{g}(j)}}{\Gamma(\tau)}\Bigg((\tau-1)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau-1\right)

\displaystyle\times

\,{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+1;-1\right)

\displaystyle+\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau\right)

\displaystyle\times

{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+2;-1\right)\Bigg)\,,

where
{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}
is the regularized hypergeometric function, the skewness
\eta
dependent spin-j
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}
-terms are also given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T},\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,-K^{2})-1\right)\times\left[\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T})-\mathcal{A}_{S}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{S}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K,\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\,.

(H.340)

and

\displaystyle\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,-K^{2})=\,_{2}F_{1}\left(-\frac{j}{2},\frac{1-j}{2};\frac{1}{2}-j;\frac{4m_{N}^{2}}{K^{2}}\times\eta^{2}\right)\,.

(H.341)

We have also defined the dimensionless scale functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}(-\Delta_{g}(j)-j+2+4)}\times\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}{Q}\Big)^{4}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\frac{\hat{\xi}^{\Delta_{g}(j)+j+2-5}}{4}\times\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\mathcal{V}_{V}(\hat{\xi}M_{V}/Q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j)+2)\right)}\times\left(\frac{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\frac{1}{4}\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j))-2\right)}\times\left(\frac{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)\times\frac{1}{4}\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j))\right)\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j))-1\right)\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j))-2\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{V})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}(-\Delta_{g}(j)-j+2+4)}\times\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}}{Q}\Big)^{4}

(H.342)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\frac{\hat{\xi}^{\Delta_{g}(j)+j+2-5}}{4}\times\partial_{\hat{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\times\partial_{\hat{\xi}}\mathcal{V}_{V}(\hat{\xi}M_{V}/Q)\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{f_{V}}{M_{V}}\times g_{5}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{V}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{g}(j)+2)\right)}\times\frac{1}{4}\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{2}{j+\Delta_{g}(j)}\right)\times\mathcal{I}(j,Q,M_{V})\,.

We also have

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}(j)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2+\sqrt{2\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0g})}\,,

(H.343)

with
j_{0g}=2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}
for closed strings.

H.9
DVCS with t-channel open string exchanges in AdS

Spin-2 vector contribution

The spin-2 vector meson contribution to the transverse holographic DVCS amplitude at finite skewness,
\eta\neq 0
,
is illustrated in Fig.
3
right.
Its explicit contribution to the
TT
holographic amplitude is

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{TT}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\gamma p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{T},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}_{T}\times\frac{1}{g_{5}^{2}}\times g_{5}^{2}\times\left(\frac{s}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}\right)^{2}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{vector}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times\frac{1}{4}

(H.344)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[A_{q}(K)+\eta^{2}D_{q}(K)\right]\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\mathcal{O}(1/N_{c})\,,

where we have defined

\displaystyle A_{q}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q}(j=2,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j=2),K)\,,

\displaystyle\eta^{2}D_{q}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}(j=2,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j=2),K)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{vector}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1/N_{c})\,,

with
\mathcal{A}_{q}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)
defined below in (
H.347
).

Spin-j vector contribution

The spin-j vector mesons contribution is given by (for even
j=2,4,...
).

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{TT}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\gamma p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{T},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon_{T}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}_{T}\times\frac{1}{g_{5}^{2}}\times g_{5}^{2}\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\times\mathcal{O}(1/N_{c})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{4^{j/2}}\times\left(\frac{s}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}\right)^{j}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{vector}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{A}_{q}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)\right]\,,

We have defined the quark spin-j form factors, for even
j=2,4,...
, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K;\tilde{\kappa}_{V})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(j\rightarrow j+1,\tau,\Delta_{g}\rightarrow\Delta_{q},K;\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\,,

(H.347)

and the quark spin-j skewness dependent
\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}
-terms as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\eta}(j\rightarrow j+1,\tau,\Delta_{g}\rightarrow\Delta_{q},K;\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\,,

(H.348)

where
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}
are given by (
H.8
) and (
H.8
), respectively. The polynomial
\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,m_{n}^{2})
is also the same as for the spin-j glueballs (for even
j=2,4,..
) (
H.341
).We have also defined the dimensionless scale factors as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{vector}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1/N_{c})\,,

(H.349)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}(j)=2+\sqrt{4+m^{2}R^{2}+\frac{R^{2}}{\alpha^{\prime}}(j-1)}=2+\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0q})}\,,

(H.350)

for even
j=2,4,...
with

. Note that

refers to open strings attached to

D9-branes (Reggeons),
which is to be contrasted with
j_{0g}=2-2/\sqrt{\lambda}
for the closed strings (Pomerons).

Spin-j axial-vector contribution

The arguments for the vector meson exchange, can be extended to the axial vector meson exchange in bulk.
In particular, the
spin-j axial meson exchange (for odd
j=1,3,...
) contribution to the DVCS amplitude is

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LT}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\gamma p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon^{\prime}_{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times g_{5}\times g_{5}^{3}\kappa_{cs}\times\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}\epsilon_{L\mu}\epsilon_{T\nu}^{\prime*}\times i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\tilde{q}_{\sigma}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2(j-1)}}s^{j-1}\times\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{axial}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{F}_{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{A\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)\right]\,,

with the Chern-Simons coupling
\kappa_{cs}=\frac{N_{c}}{24\pi^{2}}
. We have defined the spin-j axial form factors as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),t)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}(j\rightarrow j+1,\tau,\Delta_{g}\rightarrow\Delta_{q},K;\tilde{\kappa}_{T}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\times\tilde{\kappa}_{V})\,,

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}(j,\tau,\Delta_{g}(j),K)=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{1-\Delta_{g}(j)}}{\Gamma(\tau)}\Bigg((\tau-1)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau-1\right)

\displaystyle\times

\,{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+1;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+1;-1\right)

\displaystyle-\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{j-2}{2}+\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+\tau\right)

\displaystyle\times

{}_{2}\tilde{F}_{1}\left(\frac{a_{K}}{2}-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2,\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau-\frac{\Delta_{g}(j)}{2}+2;\frac{a_{K}}{2}+\frac{j-2}{2}+\tau+2;-1\right)\Bigg)\,,

and the anomalous dimension of the spin-j conformal singlet axial-vector quark operator at
\mu=\mu_{0}

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}(j)=2+\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0q})}\,,

(H.354)

with
j_{0q}=1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}
. The skewness or

\eta
-dependent spin-j
\mathcal{D}_{A\eta}
-terms are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{A\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\hat{d}_{j}(\eta,t)-1\right)\times\left[\mathcal{F}_{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K;\tilde{\kappa}_{V})-\mathcal{F}_{AS}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),t;\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\right]\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{AS}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K;\tilde{\kappa}_{S})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K;\tilde{\kappa}_{V}\rightarrow\tilde{\kappa}_{S})\,.

(H.356)

In principle, we can also calculate the scale factor
\mathcal{I}_{singlet}^{axial}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})
very precisely,
using the two virtual photon coupling to one axial meson derived in
Mamo and Zahed 2021c
. However, we do not need its precise form to extract the moments of axial spin-j (odd
j=1,3,...
) or axial singlet moments.

H.10
Pair meson electroproduction with t-channel open string exchange in AdS

Spin-1 vector meson t-exchange

The t-channel spin-1 vector meson resonances contribution to the transverse holographic pair meson production amplitude at finite skewness,
\eta\neq 0
, is illustrated in Fig.
2
right. The bulk t-exchange refers to a spin-1 meson, coupled in bulk to the bulk-to-boundary
virtual photon of momentum
q
, and a spin-2 bulk-to-boundary graviton of momentum
q^{\prime}
decaying to a
\pi\pi
pair at the boundary with momentum
p_{1\pi}
and
p_{2\pi}
respectively. Specifically, the contribution is

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi\pi p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L};m_{\pi\pi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times g_{5}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}s}\epsilon_{L\mu}p_{\nu}T^{\mu\nu}_{\pi(gluon)}(q^{\prime})\times\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{vector}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times F_{1}(K)

(H.357)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})\,,

where
T^{\mu\nu}_{\pi(gluon)}(q^{\prime})
is the gravitational form factor of the pion (or any other meson) in the time-like region, and we have defined the dimensionless functions

\displaystyle F_{1}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}(j=1,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j=1),K)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{vector}(Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\times\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}}{Q}\Big)^{5}\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\hat{\xi}^{-1}\times\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{4}}{4}\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{2}}{1}

(H.358)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\times\frac{Q^{2}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}\times\frac{225\pi}{2048}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\right)}\,,

with
\hat{\xi}\equiv Qz
, and
\mathcal{F}_{1}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)
defined below in (
H.10
).

Spin-j vector meson t-exchange

The extension of the holographic spin-1 exchange in bulk near threshold, extends to the spin-j exchange away from threshold,
using a similar Witten diagram as in Fig.
2
right. Specifically,
the spin-j vector meson exchange in bulk contribution reads

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi\pi p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L};m_{\pi\pi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times g_{5}\times 2\kappa^{2}\times\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}s}\epsilon_{L\mu}p_{\nu}T^{\mu\nu}_{\pi(gluon)}(q^{\prime})\times\frac{1}{2m_{N}}\times\bar{u}(p_{2})u(p_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2(j-1)}}s^{j-1}\times\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{vector}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{q}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{F}_{1}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}\right]\,,

for odd
j=1,3,...
. We have defined the spin-j vector form factors as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K;\tilde{\kappa}_{V})

for odd
j=1,3,...
. The spin-j vector form factors
\mathcal{A}_{q}
and
\mathcal{D}_{q\eta}
are given by (
H.347
) and (
H.348
), respectively. We have also defined the scale factors

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{vector}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\times\Big(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}}{Q}\Big)^{1+\Delta_{q}+j}\times\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}d\hat{\xi}\,e^{-\hat{\xi}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}{Q^{2}}}\,\hat{\xi}^{-1+2(j-1)}\times\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{V}_{\gamma^{*}}(\hat{\xi})\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{4}}{4}\times\frac{\hat{\xi}^{\Delta_{q}(j)-1-(j-1)}}{1}

(H.361)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\times\frac{Q^{2}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{32}\times\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}(j+\Delta_{q}(j)+3)\right)^{2}\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}}+1\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{4\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{q}(j)+j+5)}\right)}\,,

with the anomalous dimension

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}(j)=2+\sqrt{4+m^{2}R^{2}+\frac{R^{2}}{\alpha^{\prime}}(j-1)}=2+\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda}(j-j_{0q})}\,,

(H.362)

Here
m^{2}R^{2}=-3
, and
j_{0q}=1-1/\sqrt{\lambda}
for open strings attached to
N_{f}
D9 bulk filling branes.

H.11
Neutral pion electroproduction with t-channel open string exchange in AdS

Spin-j axial meson t-exchange

The holographic dual of the neutral pion production as shown in in Fig.
4
right, involves
the bulk spin-j axial meson exchange (even
j=2,4,...
)

\displaystyle{\cal A}^{LL}_{\gamma^{*}p\rightarrow\pi^{0}p}(s,t,Q,\epsilon_{L},\epsilon_{L}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\times\frac{1}{g_{5}}\times g_{5}^{2}\times\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{4}}\times\epsilon_{L}^{\prime}\cdot p\times\bar{u}(p_{2})\epsilon_{L}\cdot\gamma\,\gamma_{5}u(p_{1})

(H.363)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{\tilde{\kappa}_{N}^{2(j-2)}}s^{j-2}\times\frac{f_{\pi}}{m_{\pi}}\times g_{5}\times\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{axial}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})\times\frac{1}{\Gamma(\Delta_{va}(j)-2)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{F}_{A}(j,\tau,\Delta_{va}(j),K)+\mathcal{D}_{A\eta}(j,\tau,\Delta_{q}(j),K)\right]\,,

where the scale factor

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{valence}^{axial}(j,Q,\tilde{\kappa}_{N})=\mathcal{O}(1/N_{c})\,,

(H.364)

and
\mathcal{F}_{A}
and
\mathcal{D}_{A\eta}
are given by (
H.9
) and (
H.9
), respectively, for even
j=2,4,...
.
